# Homily on Psalm 6 (Omelia sul Salmo 6)

**Author**: Anastase le Sinaïte  
**Series**: PO Tome 52  
**Columns**: pp. 458–520  

[p. 458] {#po.52.p458}
ϯⲕⲩⲣⲓⲁⲕⲏ ⲉⲧϧⲁϫⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲟⲩⲣ ⲉϧⲟⲩⲛ
THE SUNDAY THAT IS BEFORE THE FAST Title
ⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲗⲟⲅⲟⲥ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲁⲅⲓⲟⲥ ⲓⲱⲁⲛⲛⲏⲥ ⲡⲓⲭⲣⲏⲥⲟⲥⲧⲟⲙⲟⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲡⲓⲁⲣⲭⲏⲉⲡⲓⲥⲕⲟⲡⲟⲥ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲕⲱⲥⲧⲁⲛⲧⲓⲛⲟⲩⲡⲟⲗⲓⲥ ⲉⲁϥϫⲟϥ ⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲉⲣⲙⲏⲛⲉⲩⲓⲛ ⲙⲡⲓⲙⲁϩⲥ̅ ⲙⲯⲁⲗⲙⲟⲥ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲧⲁⲟⲩⲟϥ ⲇⲉ ϧⲁϫⲉⲛ ϯⲛⲏⲥ‐ ⲧⲓⲁ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩϩⲓⲣⲏⲛⲏ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⪓ϯ ⲁⲙⲏⲛ:
A homily of Saint John Chrysostom, Archbishop of Constantinople, (that) which he pronounced interpreting the sixth Psalm; he held it before the holy fast. In the peace of God amen Repentance and forgiveness of God
ⲟⲩⲡⲉⲧⲉⲥϣⲉ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲑⲣⲉⲧⲁⲣⲭⲏ ⲛ̅ϯⲛⲏⲥⲧⲓⲁ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ϣⲱⲡⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲧⲟⲩⲃⲟ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲥⲃⲱ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓ ⲡⲛ̅ⲁ̅ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲑⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲁϩⲥ̅ ⲙⲯⲁⲗⲙⲟⲥ ⲑⲁⲓ ⲉⲧϧⲏ ⲉⲣⲣⲏⲓ ⲉⲟⲩϩⲩⲡⲟⲑⲉⲥⲓⲥ ⲙⲙⲉ‐ ⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲉⲕⲕⲗⲏⲥⲓⲁ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁϭⲓ ⲥⲃⲱ ⲅⲁⲣ ϧⲉⲛ
1 1. It is fitting that the beginning of the holy fast should take place with purity. 2. We have the instruction of the Holy Spirit which is in the sixth Psalm and which stands in all the Churches of Christ as a foundation of repentance. 3. We will learn, in fact, in this specific psalm in what way God will forgive us.
ⲡⲁⲓ ⲯⲁⲗⲙⲟⲥ ⲫⲁⲓ ϫⲉ ϧⲉⲛ ⲁϣ ⲛⲥⲙⲟⲧ ⲁⲣⲉ⪓ϯ ⲛⲁⲭⲱ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁϥ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲑⲉⲃⲓⲟ ⲛ̅ϯϫⲓⲛⲉⲣ ⲟⲙⲟⲗⲟⲅⲓⲛ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲑⲛⲁⲉⲣ ⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲑⲙⲏⲓ ⲁϭⲛⲉ ⲙⲉⲧⲛⲟⲩϫ ⲡϩⲏⲃⲓ ⲛ̅ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲡϥⲓ ⲁϩⲟⲙ ⲛ̅ϯϫⲓⲛⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲛ̅ϯⲥⲩⲛⲏ‐ ⲇⲓⲥⲓⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲥⲑⲉⲛⲏⲥ ⲑⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲑⲉⲃⲓⲟ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧϣⲟⲡ ϧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲉⲩⲟϣ ⲡⲓⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲡⲓⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲛⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⪓ϯ ϯϫⲓⲛⲥⲣⲱϥⲧ ⲉ⪓ϯ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲉϫⲱⲣϩ ϯⲉⲥⲑⲏⲥⲓⲥ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲛ̀ⲟⲩⲙⲏϣ ⲛ̅ⲥⲟⲡ ϩⲓϫⲉⲛ ⲡⲓϭⲗⲟϫ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲙⲁ ⲛ̅ⲉⲛⲕⲟⲧ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϯϫⲓⲛⲉⲣ ϧⲏⲃⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ
2 1. It contains, in fact, the humility of confessing for those who will repent sincerely without falsehood, the sorrow of tears, the sigh of conversion, the disturbance of conscience, and the weakness that humbles those who are in many sins, 2. the salvation (coming) from the mercy of God, the dedicating of oneself to God during (every) night, the perception that will often occur in bed and on the couch, and our protection by the Holy Spirit through (the) tears.
ⲡⲓ ⲡⲛⲁ̅ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲓϫⲓⲛⲥⲁⲃⲟ ⲉⲛⲓϩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲑⲙⲏⲓ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ϯϫⲓⲛⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲓ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲥⲩⲛⲭⲱⲣⲏⲥⲓⲥ ⲉⲧϫⲏⲕ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯϫⲓⲛⲉⲣ ⲁⲛⲁⲭⲱⲣⲓⲥⲓⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲉⲛϫⲁϫⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲉⲡⲓⲧⲓⲙⲓⲁ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲛⲱⲟⲩ
3 1. Learning the things (proper) to sincere repentance is not only this, but (it is) also the remission that will proceed from these, the perfect forgiveness, the withdrawal of the enemies, the reproach that will be directed at them, 2. the power
ⲛⲉⲙ ⲧϫⲟⲙ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϣⲗⲏⲗ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲉ̀ⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧϥ ⲙ̅⪓ϯ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓϣⲓⲡⲓ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲇⲉⲙⲱⲛ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲧϫⲓⲛⲉⲣ ⲡⲗⲁⲥⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓ ⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲁⲡⲁⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲛⲟⲃⲓ
of prayer that will come from God, the shame that the demons will feel through this (prayer), the salvation and the (new) formation of the man who has aged in sins.

### Exegetical section
On Ps 6:1


[p. 460] {#po.52.p460}
Ϫⲉ ⲉⲥϧⲏ ⲇⲉ ⲉⲣⲣⲏⲓ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ϯⲉⲡⲓⲅⲣⲁⲫⲏ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲯⲁⲗⲙⲟⲥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲓⲣⲏϯ ϫⲉ ⲡⲓ ⲯⲁⲗⲙⲟⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲇⲁⲩⲓⲇ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲓⲙⲁϩⲏ̅ ⲉⲣⲉϯⲅⲣⲁⲫⲏ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉⲣ ⲟⲛⲟⲙⲁⲍⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲙⲁϩⲏ̅ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲑⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲁ ⲡⲓⲍ̅ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲉⲱⲛ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲛⲱⲛϧ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ ⲉⲑⲛⲏⲟⲩ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯϫⲓⲛⲓ̀ⲛⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ⲉϫⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲥⲉⲃⲓ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲥⲉⲣ ϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲛ̅ⲉⲣ ⲥⲩⲙⲉⲛⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟϥ ⲛⲁⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲁϩⲏ̅ ⲛ̅ ⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲡⲓⲥⲟⲫⲟⲥ ⲥⲟⲗⲟⲙⲱⲛ ϯ ⲥⲃⲱ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲓⲙⲁϩⲏ̅ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ϯ ⲛ̅ⲧⲧⲟⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲁ ⲡⲓⲙⲁϩⲍ̅ ⲙ̅ⲫⲁ ⲡⲓⲙⲁϩⲏ̅ ϩⲱⲥ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ϥⲓ ⲫⲣⲱⲟⲩϣ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲍ̅ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲃⲓⲟⲥ ⲫⲁⲓ ϣⲁ ⲟⲩϣⲟ ⲛ̅
4 1. The title of this Psalm is, therefore, placed in the following manner: The Psalm of David regarding the Eighth (Ps 6:1). 2. Scripture, in fact, calls the Eighth that which is after the seventh age - that is, our eternal life that will come - and our transition to the circumcision which (Scripture) had announced to us with the eighth day (Lev 12:3; 2 Mac 10:6). 3. And precisely regarding the Eighth the wise Solomon teaches us saying: "Give a portion to those of the Seventh (and) to that of the Eighth" (Eccl 11:2), 4. as if to say: "Take care of the Seven of this life - which is a thousand years - because of the good portion of the Eight that will come and that will remain for eternal life." On Ps 6:2a
ⲉⲑⲛⲁⲙⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲟⲩⲱⲛϧ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲧⲉⲛⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲡⲓⲡⲣⲟⲟⲓⲙⲓⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲯⲁⲗⲙⲟⲥ ⲛⲟⲩⲱⲧ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ‐ ϫⲉ ⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲥⲟϩⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲉϥⲛⲁⲩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲃⲁⲗ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲟⲉⲣⲟⲛ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲁⲓⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ ϩⲱⲥ ϫⲉ ⲉϥⲟϩⲓ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ ⲉⲡⲓⲙⲁ ⲛϯ ϩⲁⲡ ⲉⲧⲟⲓ ⲛ̀ϩⲟϯ ⲉϥϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉϥⲃⲏϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲣⲉⲧⲉϥⲛⲁϩⲃⲓ ⲕⲟⲗϫ ⲉⲣⲣⲏⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϫⲉ
5 1. Let us see now the prelude of this same Psalm which says: "Lord, do not rebuke me in your anger!" (Ps 6:2a). 2. This prophet, with spiritual eyes, seeing himself standing in front of the terrible court, and there stripped, bowing his neck and having touched the feet of the judge, like one who has neither freedom of speech nor claim
ⲛϥϭⲟϩ ⲁⲛ ⲛϩⲗⲓ ⲛⲧⲟⲧϥ ⲙⲡⲓⲕⲣⲓⲧⲏⲥ ϩⲱⲥ ϫⲉ ⲉϥⲟⲓ ⲙⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲛ ⲟⲩⲁⲓ ⲉⲉϩⲙⲙⲟⲛⲧⲉϥ ⲡⲁⲣⲣⲏⲥⲓⲁ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲡⲣⲟⲥⲱⲡⲟⲛ ⲉϥⲉⲣ ϩⲏⲃⲓ ⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲉⲩⲡⲟⲣⲓⲁ ⲁⲛ ⲛ̀ⲟⲩⲁⲡⲟⲗⲟⲅⲓⲁ ⲛ ⲟⲩⲱⲧ ⲉⲧⲁⲟⲩⲟⲥ ϧⲁ ⲛⲉϥⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲛϥϭⲟϩ ⲁⲛ ⲛϩⲗⲓ ⲛⲧⲟⲧϥ ⲙⲡⲓⲣⲉϥϯ ϩⲁⲡ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙⲫⲁⲓ
regretting, and not even having a single excuse to say in defense of his sins, asks nothing of the judge, but says only this: "Lord, do not rebuke me in your anger!"
ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ϫⲉ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲥⲟϩⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕϫⲱⲛⲧ Ⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲡⲁϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲇⲓⲙⲱⲣⲓⲁ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲃⲁⲥⲁⲛⲟⲥ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲑⲏϣ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϫⲉ ⲛ̀ⲑⲟⲕ ⲉⲑⲛⲁⲧⲁϩⲟⲓ ⲉⲣⲁⲧ ⲉⲡⲉⲕⲃⲏⲙⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲉⲕⲙⲁ ⲛϯ ϩⲁⲡ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲙⲑⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲕⲧⲏⲥⲓⲥ ⲧⲏⲣⲥ ⲛⲧⲉⲕⲉⲣ ϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲛϩⲉⲙⲥⲓ ⲉⲩϩⲁⲡ ϩⲓϫⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕⲑⲣⲟⲛⲟⲥ ⲉⲧⲟⲓ ⲛ̅ϩⲟϯ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲑⲙⲉϩ ⲛⲟⲩⲱⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛⲥⲉϭⲱⲣⲡ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̀ϫⲉ ⲛⲓϩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϯⲛⲁϣⲉⲣ ⲧⲟⲗ‐ ⲙⲁⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲡⲁϭⲥ̅ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲡⲣⲟⲥⲱⲡⲟⲛ ⲉⲛⲁⲛⲉϥ ⲉⲑⲣⲓⲉⲣ ⲉⲧⲓⲛ ⲛ̅ϯⲥⲩⲛ‐
6 1. I know, my Lord, that every punishment and every torture are established for me, that you will make me appear before your tribunal and your court in the presence of the angels and of all creation, (that) you will sit first to (formulate) a judgment on your throne, terrible and full of glory, (that) all the works of my sins will be revealed and will be brought to light. 2. And I will not be able to dare, my Lord, with a just claim to ask for the complete forgiveness of my sins.


[p. 462] {#po.52.p462}
ⲭⲱⲣⲓⲥⲓⲥ ⲉⲧϫⲏⲕ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲛⲟⲃⲓ ϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲛⲓϣϯ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲁⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲕⲉⲭⲁϥ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϫⲉ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲁⲓϯ ϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲙⲡⲉⲕⲣⲁⲛ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲡⲓϣⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϫⲉ ⲁⲓⲱⲛⲣ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲧⲁⲧⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲡⲓϣⲏⲣⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲧⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϫⲉ ⲁⲓϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲉⲓⲉⲣ ⲭⲣⲉⲱⲥⲧⲓⲛ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲭⲣⲉⲱⲥⲧⲓⲛ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲛⲟⲩⲑⲃⲁ ⲛϫⲓⲛϭⲱⲣ
7 1. Because my sin is too great to be remitted, because I have sinned against you more than any (other) man, I have provoked your name beyond measure, and because I myself have lived more dissolutely than the son of dissoluteness (Lk 15:13); and because I have become your debtor and have more debts than he who had a debt with you of ten thousand talents (Mt 18:24).
ⲡⲓϫⲁϫⲓ ⲁϥⲁⲓⲧ ⲛⲁⲧϣⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲁⲕⲱⲥ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲧⲉⲗⲱⲛⲏⲥ ⲡⲓⲣⲉ‐ ϥϧⲱⲧⲉⲃ ⲁϥϧⲟⲑⲃⲉⲧ ⲕⲁⲕⲱⲥ ⲁϥⲁⲓⲧ ⲛⲥⲟⲛⲓ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲥⲟⲛⲓ
2. The enemy has badly made me viler than the publican (Lk 18:10-13). The murderer has badly killed me (and) has reduced me to a thief worse


[p. 464] {#po.52.p464}
ⲡⲓⲙⲁⲓⲡⲟⲣⲛⲟⲥ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲡⲟⲣⲛⲉⲩⲓⲛ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧϥ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ϯⲡⲟⲣⲛⲏ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲡϣⲓ ⲛϯⲙⲉⲧⲣⲉϥⲟⲩⲉⲙ ϩⲑⲏϥ ⲛⲁⲁ‐ ⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ϭⲓⲥⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲁⲫⲉ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ ⲁⲩϩⲣⲟϣ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ⲉϫⲱⲓ ⲙⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲛⲟⲩⲉⲧⲫⲱ ⲉⲥϩⲟⲣϣ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ϯⲭⲁⲛⲁⲛⲉⲟⲥ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲧⲁⲗⲉⲡⲱⲣⲓ ⲛ ⲁⲓⲕⲱⲗϫ ϣⲁ ⲣⲁⲉ ⲁⲓϯ ϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲓϯ ⲙ̅ⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲙ̅ ⲡⲉⲕⲡ̅ⲛ̅ⲁ̅ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲁⲧⲥⲱⲧⲉⲙ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲛⲉⲕⲉⲛⲧⲟⲗⲏ ⲁⲓϫⲱⲣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧⲣⲁⲙⲁⲟ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲁⲕⲱⲥ ⲁⲓϭⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲁⲕⲱⲥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲁϩⲟ ⲁⲓⲧⲁⲕⲟ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲁ̀ⲣⲣⲏⲃ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲁⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲧⲏⲓϥ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲁⲓϭϧⲱϧⲉⲙ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲉⲣⲫⲉⲓ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲡⲁⲥⲱⲙⲁ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲕⲁⲧⲁϩⲓ ⲕⲱⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲧⲁ‐ ⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲧⲉ ⲁⲓⲑⲟⲗⲃⲉⲥ ⲁⲓⲁⲓⲥ ⲛⲁⲧϣⲁⲩ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲟⲩⲃ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲧⲏⲓϥ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲁⲓⲱⲛⲣ ⲛ̀ⲣⲏⲧϥ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲥⲙⲏ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲉⲕⲉⲛⲧⲟⲗⲏ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲁⲣⲉϩ ⲉⲣⲟⲥ ⲧⲉⲕϩⲉⲃⲥⲱ ⲑⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲧⲏⲓⲥ ϩⲓⲱⲧ ⲁⲓⲑⲟⲗⲃⲉⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩϭⲱϧⲉⲙ ϯⲗⲁⲙⲡⲁⲥ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲥⲉⲃⲧⲱⲧⲥ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲁⲓϭⲉⲛⲟⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩϩⲓ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲁⲡⲣⲟⲥⲱ‐ ⲡⲟⲛ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲁⲓϥ ⲛⲟⲩⲱⲓⲛⲓ ⲁⲓϯ ϣⲓⲡⲓ ⲛⲁϥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲫⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲛⲁⲃⲁⲗ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲑⲣⲟⲩⲉⲣ ⲟⲩⲱⲓⲛⲓ ⲁⲓⲁⲓⲧⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ ⲃⲉⲗⲗⲉ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲁⲕⲱⲥ ⲛⲁⲥⲫⲟⲧⲟⲩ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲧⲟⲩⲃⲱⲟⲩ ⲁⲓⲑⲟⲗⲃⲟⲩ
than the thief (Lk 10:30). The fornicator, by means of him, I have fornicated more than the prostitute (Lk 7:37; Jn 8:3-11). I have committed sin beyond the measure of repentance. 3. "My iniquities have surpassed my head" (Ps 37:5) more than Manasseh (2 Kgs 21:1-18). "They have weighed upon me, like a heavy burden" (Ps 37:5) more than upon the Canaanite woman (Mt 15:22-28). I have lamented and in the end I have bowed down (Ps 37:7). 8 1. I have provoked you, Lord, with my sins and I have made your Holy Spirit sad (Eph 4:30). I have disobeyed your commandments. 2. I have badly dispersed your wealth. I have badly wasted your treasure (Col 2:2-3). With my iniquities I have destroyed your pledge that you had given me. I have stained your holy temple, that is, my body (1 Cor 6:19). 3. That which is according to your image (Gen 1:26), that is, my soul, I have dirtied and I have made it vile. Your gold that you had given me, I have dissipated by living thanks to this together with my enemies. The voice of your commandments I have not observed. Your garment that you had put upon me (Gen 3:21), I have dirtied with a stain (Rev 3:4). The lamp that you had prepared for me, I have extinguished by dozing off (Mt 25:1-13). 4. My face that you had made luminous, I have brought to shame with sin. My eyes that you had made shine, I have badly made blind (Mt 6:22-23). My lips that you had purified, I have dirtied.
ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϯⲉ̀ⲙⲓ ϫⲉ ⲭⲛⲁⲧⲁϩⲟⲓ ⲉⲣⲁⲧ ⲉⲡⲉⲕⲃⲏⲙⲁ ⲉⲓⲃⲏϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϯⲉⲙⲓ ϫⲉ ⲭⲛⲁⲥⲟϩⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ⲡⲁⲛⲧⲱⲥ ⲉϫⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϫⲉ ϯⲛⲁ‐ ϣⲫⲱⲧ ⲁⲛ ⲥⲁ ⲧϫⲟⲙ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲣⲥⲟϩⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϩⲱ ϯⲛⲁϫⲉⲙ ϩⲏⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧⲕ ⲡⲓⲙⲁⲓ ⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ
9 1. I know that, stripped bare, you will make me appear before your tribunal. I know that you will certainly reproach me for my sins, that I will not be able to flee from this, and that I will certainly be reproached. Indeed, it is impossible (to flee this). 2. But 'do not reproach me in your anger!' The same will instead profit from this on your part, the only philanthropist.
ⲕⲉⲙⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲑⲙⲏⲓ ⲉⲛⲁⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲉⲧϩⲏⲡ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲥⲟϩⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ Ϫ ⲙⲡⲉⲣϩⲓ ⲱⲓϣ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ Ⲛⲟⲩⲙⲏϣ ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲟⲩⲟⲛϩⲟⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲁⲅ‐ ⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲉⲩϣⲓⲡⲓ ⲛⲏⲓ
3. You truly know all my hidden sins, but do not reproach me, do not make me known to a crowd, (and) do not reveal them to all the angels and to all men to my shame.


[p. 466] {#po.52.p466}
ⲡϭⲥ̅ ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲥⲟϩⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲡϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲛ̀ⲟⲩⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲉϣⲁϥⲙⲟⲩ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ϣϫⲟⲙ ⲛ̅ϩⲗⲓ ⲉϥⲁⲓ ⲣⲁⲣⲟϥ ⲡⲱⲥ ⲙⲁⲗⲗⲟⲛ ⲡⲉⲕϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲙⲡⲁⲣⲉϯⲕⲧⲏⲥⲓⲥ ⲧⲏⲣⲥ ϣϥⲁⲓ ⲣⲁⲣⲟϥ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲥⲟϩⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛ̀ϩⲣⲏⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕⲙⲃⲟⲛ ⲙⲡⲉⲣϯ ⲥⲃⲱ ⲛⲏⲓ ϯϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ⲁⲛ ϫⲉ ⲙⲡⲉⲣϯ ⲥⲃⲱ ⲛⲏⲓ ϯⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲛⲥⲃⲱ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲕⲟⲗⲁⲥⲓⲥ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲥⲃⲱ ⲙⲡⲉⲣϯ ⲥⲃⲱ ⲛⲏⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕⲙⲃⲟⲛ ϯⲥⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲟⲩⲥⲟⲛⲓ ⲉⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲉⲧⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲕ ⲁϥϭⲓ ⲛⲟⲩⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧⲕ ϯⲥⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲡⲟⲣⲛⲏ ⲉⲁⲥϯ ⲙⲡⲉⲥⲉⲟⲩⲟⲟⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉⲥⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲧⲏⲣⲥ ⲁⲥϭⲓ ⲛⲟⲩⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϯⲥⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲧⲉⲗⲱⲛⲏⲥ ⲉⲁϥϥⲓ ⲁϩⲟⲙ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡϣⲱⲕ
4. 'Lord, do not reproach me in your anger!' If there is no possibility of sustaining the anger of a mortal king, how much less will be the possibility that all creation can sustain your anger? On Ps 6:2b 10 1. 'Lord, do not reproach me in your anger and do not instruct me in your wrath!' (Ps 6:2) 2. I do not say: 'Do not instruct me'; I deserve, in fact, every instruction and every punishment. Yet, while instructing me, do not instruct me in your wrath. 3. I know a thief who asked for and received forgiveness from you (Lk 23:40-43). I know a prostitute who had recourse to you with all her soul and obtained forgiveness (Lk 7:44-48). I know a tax collector who sighed from the depths of his heart and was justified (Lk 18:13-14).
ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥϩⲏⲧ ⲁϥⲑⲙⲁⲓⲟ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ϯⲟⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲟⲩⲣⲏϯ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛϩⲁⲛⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲩϫⲏϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛϯⲉⲝⲟⲙⲟⲗⲟⲅⲓ‐ ⲥⲓⲥ ⲙⲙⲉⲑⲙⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϥⲓ ⲁϩⲟⲙ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡϣⲱⲕ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁϩⲏⲧ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̀ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲉⲥⲧⲟⲩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲧ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̀ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲛϯⲛⲏⲥⲧⲓⲁ ⲉⲥⲧⲟⲩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲧ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ϯⲁⲅⲁⲡⲏ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲥⲟⲛ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̀ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ϯⲙⲉⲧϩⲏⲕⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲡ̅ⲛ̅ⲁ̅ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϣⲗⲏⲗ ⲉⲧⲙⲏⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛϯⲙⲉⲧⲣⲉϥϣⲉⲡ ϧⲓⲥⲓ ϫⲉ ⲛⲧⲁϣⲉⲡ ϧⲓⲥⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲕⲉⲟⲩⲁⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϥⲓ ⲁϩⲟⲙ ϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲧⲟⲩⲭⲱ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛϯⲥⲱϥⲣⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ϧⲉⲛ
11 1. But I am not like them. 2. I have, in fact, no tears shed. I do not possess a sincere confession. I do not have the sigh from the depths of my heart. I do not have my soul pure. I do not have pure fasting. I do not have charity towards a brother (Mt 22:39//). I do not have poverty in spirit (Mt 5:3). I do not have continuous prayer (1 Thess 5:17). I do not have (com)passion so as to pity another (Sir 7:34). I do not have the sigh so as to be able to be forgiven. I do not have moderation in my flesh. I do not have the purity of the angels. I do not have the discernment that is worthy of God.
ⲧⲁⲥⲁⲣⲝ̅ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲧⲟⲩⲃⲟ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ϯⲇⲓⲁⲕⲣⲓⲥⲓⲥ ⲉⲧⲉⲣ ⲡⲣⲉⲡⲏ ⲙⲪϯ ⲣⲉⲛ ⲁϣ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ϩⲟⲓ ⲉ ϧⲉⲛ ⲁϣ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲣⲣⲏⲥⲓⲁ ⲉⲓⲛⲁⲉⲣ ⲉⲧⲓⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲟⲩⲙⲏϣ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛ̅ⲥⲟⲡ ⲡⲁϭⲥ̅ ⲁⲓⲥⲉⲙⲛⲏⲧⲥ ⲛⲉⲙⲁⲕ ⲉⲉⲣ ⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲓϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲛ̅ⲣⲉϥϫⲉ ⲙⲉⲑⲛⲟⲩϫ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲁⲕ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲥⲩⲛ‐ ⲧⲁⲕⲏ. ⲟⲩⲙⲏϣ ⲛ̅ⲥⲟⲡ ⲁⲓϯ ϩⲟ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ϧⲉⲛ ϯⲉⲕⲕⲗⲏⲥⲓⲁ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲣϧⲉⲛ ϯⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩ ⲁⲓ̈ϣⲁⲛⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϣⲁⲓϩⲉⲓ ⲉϧⲣⲏⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲁⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ⲟⲩⲏⲣ
12 1. With what claim or with what freedom of speech shall I ask for forgiveness? 2. Many times, in fact, my Lord, I have promised you to repent and (then) I have turned out to be a liar towards you regarding the promise. Many times I have supplicated you in church and, as soon as I go out, I plunge into iniquities. 3.


[p. 468] {#po.52.p468}

How many times have you had pity on me and I, instead, have rejected you? How many times have you been patient with me, and I have provoked you again? How many times have you given me strength and I, instead, have not returned? How many times have you raised me and I have fallen again? How many times have you cured me and I, instead, have been ungrateful towards you? How many times have you listened to me and I, instead, have been disobedient towards you? How many times have you had compassion on me and I, (instead), have not served you at all? How many times have you appreciated me and I, instead, have despised you with my transgressions? 4. How many times have I committed sin and I have supplicated you as a father and you have lifted me up as a good one? 5. You gave me a kiss as (one does with a) son, you spoke with me as Savior, you called me as a little child and you stretched out your arms towards me saying to me

13 1. 'My son, why are you crying out towards me? Get up! Do not be afraid! Get back on your feet once more, come, do not be afraid! 2. I do not pursue you, I do not trouble you, I will not make my creatures abominable, I will not cast away my creation, I will not make my bowels hard towards my son. 3. I cannot hate the return to me of my image. I cannot hate the man whom I fashioned with my hands, the one whom I created, the one whom I honored (Gn 1:26-28), the one whom I carried having humbled myself for him (Phil 2:7-8). I poured out my blood for him (Mt 26:28//). 4. How (will I) not accept to me him who has returned to me and has supplicated me?'

14 1. For this, my Lord, have pity on me (Ps 50:1) in your goodness - since you have the root of your philanthropy without limit planted inside you - and in the sea of your patience. 'Do not reproach me in your anger and do not instruct me in your wrath!' (Ps 6:2), but be patient with me one more time again. 2. Do not hasten to gather me, I have lived still little. Do not hasten to cut me down like a fig tree devoid of fruit (Lk 13:6-9), but as a good God and as a philanthropic Lord grant grace to me and to anyone

else of this year still for (our) repentance. 3. Do not grow weary in bearing my negligence. Do not snatch me while I am not ready, do not take my lamp not lit (Mt 25:1-13//). Do not bring me to the wedding when I do not have the garment (Mt 22:11-13). Do not make my soul appear stripped bare, Lord, before your tribunal. Do not take me away until I have nothing to bring to you. 4. But be patient with me, but wait, but act as a philanthropist, but suffer with me,

15 1. I, the poor, the stripped bare, the negligent, the inattentive, the miserable, the ignorant, the fornicator, the one who is impure, the merciless, the dissolute, the ungrateful, the hardened of heart, the one who is (sunk) in the deep (abyss), the one who is in secret, the one who is full of shame and has no claim and has no word to say, the one who is unworthy of any philanthropy, the one who is unworthy both of heaven and of earth, the one who deserves every punishment and Gehenna and retribution. On Ps 6:3a

16 1. But, 'Lord, do not reproach me in your anger' and 'do not instruct me in your wrath!' (Ps 6:2). 'Have pity on me, Lord, because I am weak!' (Ps 6:3a) 2. I am weak in my soul. I am weak in my flesh. My reason is weak. My reasoning is weak. 3. My strength has ceased (Ps 37:11). My time has ceased. My days have ceased in vanity (Ps 77:33). I see my end, it has approached.

If, in fact, you close, who will be able to open? If you do not approach, who will be able to


[p. 472] {#po.52.p472}
ⲡⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲣⲱⲛⲧ ⲉϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲉϣⲱⲡ ⲁⲕϣⲧⲉⲙⲓⲏⲥ ⲛ̀ⲑⲟⲕ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲉⲣ ⲃⲟⲏⲑⲓⲛ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ: ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ϩⲗⲓ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲛ̀ⲑⲟⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲓⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉϥ‐ ⲫⲩⲥⲓⲥ ⲙⲟⲓ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲱⲛϩ ⲙⲟⲓ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲗⲟⲅⲓⲥ‐ ⲙⲟⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯϫⲓⲛⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟ ⲛ̀ⲑⲟⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϭⲣⲟ ⲉⲡⲁⲧϩⲉⲙⲕⲟ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛ̀ⲑⲟⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϯ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲛ̀ⲧⲁⲇⲓⲟⲣⲑⲱⲥⲓⲥ ⲙⲟⲓ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲛ̀ⲑⲟⲕ ⲛ̅ⲕⲉⲕⲟⲩϫⲓ ⲛⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲣⲓⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲛⲉⲙⲏⲓ ⲛ̀ⲑⲟⲕ ⲙ̅ⲡⲥⲙⲟⲧ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉ‐ ϣⲁⲓⲁⲓⲧⲟⲩ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲥⲉϣⲟϣϥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲥⲉⲙⲟⲧⲉⲛ ⲛ̀ⲃⲱⲗ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉϣⲁⲓϩⲓ ⲧⲟⲧ ⲉⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲥⲉⲉⲣ ⲛⲟϥⲣⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲥⲉϫⲏⲕ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲛ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲙⲡⲉⲕϩⲓⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲡⲁϭⲥ̅ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲣⲱⲛⲧ ⲉϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲡⲉⲕⲡⲗⲁⲥⲙⲁ ⲛ̀ⲑⲟⲕ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲁⲕϫⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ϣϫⲟⲙ ⲙⲙⲱⲧⲉⲛ ⲉⲉⲣ ϩⲗⲓ ⲛϩⲱⲃ
approach? If you do not hasten, who will be able to help me (Ps 69:2)? No one! 3. But you are a philanthropist precisely by your nature. Give us the time of life. Give us the time of reasoning and of conversion. It will be you who will triumph over my contrition and it will be you who will grant me repentance. You, give me still a little time. You, work with me (according to) the way of salvation. 4. Everything that I do is despicable and is easily annulled. What I undertake is not useful and is not perfect, yet, my Lord, you have not thrown me away. Approach then to your creature. You, in fact, have said: You cannot do anything without me (Jn 15:5). Obtain first from me the conversion of my soul during the time of my life.
ⲁⲣⲛⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲁⲣⲓ ϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲛ̅ϭⲓ ⲛ̀ⲧⲟⲧ ⲛ̅ϯϫⲓⲛⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟ ⲛ̀ⲧⲉ ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲙ̅ⲡⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲱⲛϧ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲧⲩⲣⲁⲛⲛⲟⲥ ⲉⲡⲓϩⲟⲩⲟ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲧⲁⲥⲩⲛⲏⲇⲏⲥⲓⲥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲟⲛⲏ‐ ⲣⲟⲛ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̀ϫⲉ ⲡⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲩϩⲱⲙⲓ ⲉϫⲱⲓ ⲛϫⲉ ⲛⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲁⲩⲁⲓⲧ ⲛϫⲱⲃ ⲟⲩⲟϩⲁⲩⲧϩⲉⲙⲕⲟⲓ ⲡⲓϫⲱⲃ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲧⲧϩⲉⲉⲙϩⲕⲏⲟⲩⲧ ⲙ ⲡⲁϥϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲛ̀ⲧⲁⲗϭⲟϥ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲡⲓϫⲱⲃ ⲙⲡⲁϥϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲛⲧⲟⲩⲛⲟⲥϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲡⲉⲧⲧϩⲉⲙⲕⲏⲟⲩⲧ ⲙ ⲡⲁϥϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲛⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲁϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ Ϫ̅ ⲡⲉⲧⲧϩⲉⲙⲕⲏⲟⲩⲧ ⲙⲡⲁϥϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲛⲉⲣ ⲃⲟⲏ ⲑⲓⲛ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲙⲡⲁϥϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲙⲙⲟⲣϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲙⲡⲁϥϣϫⲉⲙ
18 1. I am tyrannized more by my bad habit. For this, 'have pity on me, Lord, because I am weak!' (Ps 6:3a). My enemy has loosened my bones and all my enemies have trampled me, they have made me weak and have left me contrite. 2. In fact, he who is weak and contrite cannot heal himself. The weak cannot get up by himself. He who is contrite cannot have pity on himself. He who is contrite cannot help himself, cannot bandage himself, cannot wash himself.

### On Ps 6:3b
ϫⲟⲙ ⲛⲓⲁϥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲟⲩⲁⲥⲑⲉⲛⲏⲥ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲁⲛⲁⲕⲁⲥ ⲁⲩϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲁⲩϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲩⲟⲩⲟϣⲟⲩⲉϣ ⲛ̀ϫⲉ ⲛⲓⲕⲁⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲣⲉⲛⲉϥⲕⲁⲥ ⲟⲩⲉϣⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁϥϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲛ̀ⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ⲉϣⲓⲛⲓ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲡⲓⲥⲏⲓⲛⲓ Ⲙⲙⲡⲁϥϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲛ̅ⲫⲱⲧ ⲉⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ⲉⲛⲉ‐ ϥϫⲁϫⲓ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ϣⲓⲛⲓ ⲛⲥⲱⲓ ⲛⲑⲟⲕ ⲡⲁϭⲥ̅ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲑⲛⲏⲟⲩ ⲉⲕⲱϯ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ⲙⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲥⲱⲣⲉⲙ ⲛⲑⲟⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲧⲁϩⲟ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ
19 1. (Therefore), 'have pity on me, Lord, because I am weak! Heal me, Lord, because my bones have been shaken!' (Ps 6:3) 2. The bones of my soul have been shaken and have been struck down. He whose bones are struck down cannot get up to seek the physician (and) cannot flee to save himself from his enemies. 3. Then, seek me yourself, my Lord, who come 'to seek and to save that which was lost' (Lk 19:10). It is you who


[p. 474] {#po.52.p474}
ϣⲁⲣⲟⲕ ⲙⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲉⲣ ϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲛϩⲉⲓ ⲉⲧⲟⲧⲟⲩ ⲛⲛⲓⲥⲟⲛⲓ ⲉⲛⲁⲓⲟⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲙ ⲫⲁϣⲙⲟⲩ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲁⲩϧⲱⲧⲉⲃ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟⲓ ⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϯⲛⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲙⲁϩⲙⲉⲧ ϯⲛⲁⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ
have raised towards you that which before had fallen into the hands of thieves. I was not half-dead, but I have been killed (Lk 10:30). Then, 'heal me, Lord, and I will be healed, save me (and) I will be saved' (Jer 17:14).
ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲁⲡⲓϩⲟϥ ⲉⲧϩⲱⲟⲩ ⲁⲓⲧ ⲛϫⲱⲃ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁϥⲁⲓⲧ ⲛ ϣⲟⲗϩⲥ ⲁϥⲗⲉϥⲗⲱϥⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ “ ⲡⲓϫⲱⲃ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲧⲟⲓ ⲛϣⲟⲗϩⲥ ϣⲁϥⲭⲁϥ ⲉⲣϧⲣⲏⲓ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ϩⲓϫⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲕⲁϩⲓ ϣⲁϥϩⲓⲧϥ ⲉⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ϣⲁϥⲧϩⲉⲙⲕⲟϥ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲙⲏϯ ϫⲉ ϣⲁϥⲙⲟⲩϯ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲉⲡⲓⲥⲏⲓⲛⲓ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉϥⲥⲙⲏ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϣⲁϥⲱϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲉϥϣⲓ̀ⲛⲓ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲑⲛⲁⲛⲁϩⲙⲉϥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϣⲁϥϥⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉϥ ⲃⲁⲗ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲉϥⲥⲟⲙⲥ ϫⲉ ⲁϥⲛⲁⲓ̈ ⲛⲑⲛⲁⲩ ⲓⲉ ϫⲉ ⲁϥⲛⲁϫⲉⲙ ⲡⲉϥϣⲓⲛⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ⲡⲉⲑⲙⲟⲩⲣ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲑⲧϩⲉⲙⲕⲏⲟⲩⲧ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲧⲃⲱⲗ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ⲛⲏ
20 1. Heal me, Lord, because the evil serpent (Gn 3) has made me completely weak, has reduced me to a corpse and has destroyed me. 2. In fact, the weak and corpse places all of himself on the ground, throws himself completely to the ground, is all contrite, unless he calls the physician with his voice and cries out seeking him who will save him, or lifts his eyes towards the heights seeing when he will come or when he will make his visit, 3. he who bandages the contrite (Ps 146:3) and who loosens those who are bound (Lk 4:18) and who raises the fallen (Ps 144:14) and who saves the desperate!
ⲉⲧⲥⲟⲛϩ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲧⲧⲁϩⲟ ⲉⲣⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲛ̀ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲁⲩϩⲉⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲧⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ⲛ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲁⲓⲁⲧⲟⲧⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲥⲱⲟⲩ ⲉⲓⲱϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲉⲓϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟⲓ ⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲛⲓⲕⲁⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲁⲩϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ϯϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ⲡⲁϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲁⲟⲩϣⲑⲟⲣ‐ ⲧⲉⲣ ⲉϥⲟϣ ⲧⲟⲛⲱ ⲧⲁϩⲟⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲁⲥⲱⲙⲁ ⲁⲓϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ϫⲉ ⲁⲓϩⲉⲓ ⲉⲣⲣⲏⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲡⲁⲑⲟⲥ ⲛ̅ⲥⲁⲣⲕⲓⲕⲟⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲙ̅ⲯⲩⲭⲓⲕⲟⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲧⲁⲥⲁⲣⲝ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲛⲥⲱⲃⲓ ⲛ ⲛⲓⲇⲉⲙⲱⲛ
21 1. I am crying out towards him when I say: 'Heal me, Lord, because the bones of my soul are shaken.' I say it, my Lord: They are shaken to the limit. Support my body. 2. I have been troubled because I have fallen down into carnal and spiritual passions and I have reduced my flesh and my soul to being a plaything of the demons.
“ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲁⲩϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲛϫⲉ ⲛⲓⲥⲛⲁⲩϩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲁⲕⲁⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛⲓⲥⲟϩⲓ ⲛⲛⲁ ⲡⲁⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲉⲧⲥⲁϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲁϣ ⲇⲉ ⲛⲉ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲡⲓ ⲛⲁϩϯ ⲡⲓⲙⲉⲩⲓ ϯϩⲉⲗⲡⲓⲥ ϯⲁⲅⲁⲡⲏ ϯⲥⲟⲫⲣⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ϯⲉⲅⲣⲁⲧⲓⲁ ϯⲇⲓⲕⲉⲟⲥⲩⲛⲏ ϯⲙⲉⲧϣⲁⲙϣⲉ ⲛⲟⲩϯ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲣⲉⲙⲣⲁⲩϣ ⲡⲓⲑⲉⲃⲓⲟ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲉ ⲛⲓⲕⲁⲥ ⲉⲧⲁⲩⲣⲱϫⲡ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲩⲣϧⲟⲙϧⲉⲙ ⲉϫⲱⲓ ⲉⲡⲓⲇⲏ ⲛⲉⲕⲑⲙⲏⲓ ⲡ̅ϭⲥ̅ ⲭⲛⲁⲁⲣⲉϩ ⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲕⲁⲥ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ϫⲉ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲟⲩⲁⲓ ⲗⲟϥⲗⲉϥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ϧⲏⲧⲟⲩ ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲁⲩⲣⲟⲙϧⲉⲙ ⲛϫⲉ ⲛⲓⲕⲁⲥ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲡⲥⲱⲙⲁ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲕⲙⲁⲣⲧⲩⲣⲟⲥ
22 1. Heal me, Lord, because the bonds of my bones and the straightenings of those of my inner man have been upset. 2. Therefore, what are they? Faith, wisdom, hope, charity, prudence, temperance, justice, the devotion of God, meekness, humility. These are the bones that have been thrown and crushed upon me. 3. Then (as for) your righteous ones, Lord, you will guard their entire bones, so that not even one of them is broken (Ps 33:2) even if the bones of the body of your martyrs have been crushed.


[p. 476] {#po.52.p476}

### On Ps 6:4a
ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲕⲁⲥ ⲁⲩϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲁⲥϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲉⲙⲁϣⲱ ϯⲛⲁⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ϫⲉ ⲁⲥⲕⲏⲛ ⲉⲉⲣ ⲡⲣⲟⲕⲟⲡⲧⲓⲛ ⲛϫⲉ ϯⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲡⲁⲱⲛϩ ⲁϥⲙⲟⲩⲛⲕ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲁⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲁⲥϧⲱⲛⲧ ⲉⲣⲟⲩⲛ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲧⲁⲣⲁⲓ ⲏ̅ ⲁⲥⲁⲓⲁⲓ ⲉⲙⲁϣⲱ ⲛϫⲉ ⲧⲁⲙⲉⲧⲣⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲡⲁⲱⲥⲣϥ ⲁϥϧⲱⲛⲧ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁϥⲓ ⲉⲡⲓⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲛ̀ⲟⲥⲣϥ ⲡⲁⲣⲉϥⲱⲥⲣ ⲓⲏⲥ ⲉϥⲧⲁⲙⲟ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ⲉⲡⲓⲟⲥⲣ ϥⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ϧⲁ ⲡⲓⲕⲉⲗⲉⲃⲓⲛ ϥⲉⲣ ⲥⲩⲛⲙⲉⲛⲓⲛ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲙⲡⲁⲱⲥⲣ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲉ
23 1. 'Heal me, Lord, because my bones have been shaken, and my soul has been troubled to the limit' (Ps 6:3b-4a). 2. Furthermore I see, in fact, that the time of my life has already gone forward, my time has failed, my end has approached, my old age is very advanced, 3. my harvest has approached, it has joined with the time of the reaping, my reaper is hurrying showing me the sickle, he lifts the axe, and gives the sign of my harvest (Mt 13:30, 39-42) to me - to him in whom there is no fruit - and of throwing me down into the fire (Mt 3:10//; 7:19).

24 1. 'And my soul has been troubled to the limit' (Ps 6:4a). I see that the things in which I find myself are not firm but are always getting worse. 'And my soul has been troubled to the limit.' I see then the thief, he has approached me (Mt 24:43//) and he hastens to snatch my life. 'And my soul has been troubled to the limit.' I see the way of my exit from this life towards the way to the afterlife painful and very far. 2. I see the creditor, he has approached me, while I am negligent in everything. I see the deadline (of the debt) already completed and this has put me in anguish. I see the auditor of accounts shaking my register of debts (Col 2:14) and the public tax collectors gnashing their teeth against me (Mt 8:12; 13:42, 50). I see the multitudes of accusers and I have no excuse to say.

25 1. 'And my soul has been troubled to the limit' (Ps 6:4a). I am thrown down. I am suffering. I am trembling and I am troubled. 2. What will I do? I do not know. I ask for a moment of life but I am afraid of increasing (still) my sins (and of) leaving without being ready. And with what claim will I see the face of the judge? I am incapable (of knowing), because I see myself among evil counsels.


[p. 478] {#po.52.p478}
ϥⲕⲏⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲉϥϯ ⲛⲉⲙⲏⲓ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲡⲟⲛⲏⲣⲟⲥ ⲥⲉⲕⲏⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲩϯ ⲛⲉⲙⲏⲓ ⲛϫⲉ ⲛⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ϥⲕⲏⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲉϥϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ⲛ̀ϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲡⲟⲗⲉⲙⲟⲥ ⲉⲧⲟⲓ ⲛϣⲫⲏⲣ ⲉⲧⲁⲥⲁⲣⲝ ⲥⲉⲱⲣϥ ⲙⲙⲱⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲛϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲗⲟⲅⲓⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲉⲧϩⲱⲟⲩ ⲛⲑⲟⲕ ⲇⲉ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϣⲁ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉ: ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲥⲩⲛⲙⲉⲛⲓⲛ ⲛⲧⲉϥϫⲟⲥ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲡⲁⲛⲧⲱⲥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲣϧⲓⲥⲓ ⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲟⲩⲱ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲧⲟⲗⲙⲏⲣⲓⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲥⲑⲉⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲉϫⲟⲥ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ Ⲫϯ ⲉⲁϥⲁⲙⲟⲛⲓ ⲉϫⲱϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲙⲡⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲧⲟⲗⲙⲁⲛ ⲉⲉⲛϥ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲁϥ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲉⲧⲁϥϫⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲑⲟⲕ ⲡϭⲥ̅
3. The evil one does not stop fighting against me. My enemies do not move away fighting against me. The war that is a companion of my flesh does not refrain from troubling me. My evil thoughts do not calm down. On Ps 6:4b 26 1. 'But you, Lord, how long?' (Ps 6:4b). 2. Why (the prophet) has not (signaled it) and has not pronounced it? But certainly for the discouragement and the suffering, he has spoken with boldness and with trembling to say it before God. Having restrained himself, he did not dare to bring himself before Him, but said precisely: 'You, Lord, how long?' (Ps 6:4b):
ϣⲁ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉ ⲓⲥ ϩⲏⲡⲡⲉ ⲭⲛⲁⲩ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ⲉⲛⲏ ⲉϯⲛ̅ ⲣⲏⲧⲟⲩ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲁⲡⲟⲣⲓⲁ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲙⲙⲉⲧⲉⲃⲓⲏⲛ ⲓⲥ ϩⲏⲡⲡⲉ ⲭⲛⲁⲩ ⲉϯϫⲓⲛⲟϩⲓ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ ⲟⲩⲏⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯϫⲓⲛⲟⲑϩⲓ ⲉⲣⲁⲧⲥ ⲉϫⲉⲛ ϯϩⲣⲱ ⲉⲧⲙⲟϩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲧⲁⲥⲁⲣⲝ̅ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲧⲁϩⲟ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲡⲟⲗⲉⲙⲟⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲓⲗⲟⲅⲓⲥⲙⲟⲥ ⲡϫⲱϫⲉⲃ ⲙⲡⲓⲭⲣⲟⲛⲟⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲣϧⲓⲥⲓ ⲛⲧⲁϫⲟⲙ
27 1. 'Behold you know, Lord, all the things in which I find myself and every miserable (anguish of mine). Behold, (You) see, in fact, the rising of my enemy and the remaining standing of my flesh in the furnace that burns (Mt 13:42, 50), and the rising of the war and of the thoughts (and) the diminution of the time, and the exhaustion of my power.'
ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ϣⲁ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ϫⲉ ⲭⲛⲁϭⲓ ⲙⲡϣⲓϣ ⲁⲛ ϣⲁ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ⲭⲛⲁⲛⲁⲩ ⲁⲛ ϣⲁ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ⲭⲛⲁⲱⲟⲩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲁⲛ ϣⲁ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ⲭⲛⲁⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟⲕ ⲁⲛ ϣⲁ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ⲭⲛⲁϫⲟⲩϣⲧ ⲁⲛ ϣⲁ ⲑⲛⲁⲩ ⲭⲛⲁⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ⲁⲛ ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϯⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲛⲭⲁⲧ ⲛⲥⲱⲕ ⲛⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ϯⲧⲱⲃϩ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲧⲁϫⲓⲛⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧⲁⲧϫⲟⲩϣⲧ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲥⲛⲁ- ϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲛⲟⲩϩⲉⲓ
2. Then, how long will you not avenge yourself? How long will you not see? How long will you not be patient? How long will you not return? How long will you not look at (me)? How long will you not free (me)? 3. As much as I deserve to be abandoned by you at every time, nevertheless, for your mercy, (I) supplicate (you) for my remission. Your neglect, in fact, will become for us (the cause of) a fall.'


[p. 480] {#po.52.p480}

28 1. (Wishing) to say before God these and other things, the prophet reflected and restrained himself so that he would not be discovered arguing and making a case against God. On Ps 6:5
ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲁϥⲫⲱⲛϩ ⲙⲡⲓⲥⲁϫⲓ ⲉⲟⲩϫⲓⲛⲧⲱⲃϩ ⲉϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ Ⲫϯ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ϫⲉ ⲕⲟⲧⲕ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲫⲩⲭⲏ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲕⲁⲧⲁ ⲛⲓϩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲟⲛ ⲡⲉ ⲙⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲛⲛⲉϥⲕⲉⲉⲧⲏⲙⲁ ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲓⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲓ̀ⲛⲓ ⲙⲙⲱⲟⲩ ⲉϫⲱϥ ⲁϥϫⲟⲥ ⲙⲡⲁⲓⲣⲏϯ ⲥⲁ ⲡϣⲱⲓ ϫⲉ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟⲓ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲱⲥ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟⲓ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲱⲥ ⲛⲁϩⲙⲉⲧ
29 1. Then, starting from this, he turned to God words of supplication (and) said: 'Turn, Lord, and free my soul. Make me live for your mercy' (Ps 6:5). 2. According to the works of the Lord, also this is like his other requests, since the prophet addresses them to Him. (In fact), he said before in this way: 'Have pity on me', 'heal me', then 'lead me back (to you)', then 'free me', 'save me'.
ⲙⲁⲧⲟⲩϫⲟⲓ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲏⲓ ϩⲱⲥ ⲁⲅⲁⲑⲟⲥ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲗϭⲟⲓ ϩⲱⲥ ⲥⲏⲓⲛⲓ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟⲓ ϩⲱⲥ ⲙⲁⲛⲉⲥⲱⲟⲩ ⲛⲣⲉϥϭⲓ ⲙⲱⲓⲧ ⲛⲁϩⲙⲉⲧ ϩⲱⲥ ϫⲱⲣⲓ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ϩⲱⲥ ⲥⲱⲧⲏⲣ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣϧⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲛⲁϩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲁⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲡⲟⲛⲏⲣⲟⲛ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲉ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲛⲁⲣϧⲓⲥⲓ ⲁⲛ ϩⲁⲛϫⲱⲃ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲉ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲛⲁⲥⲁϫⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲥⲉϣⲟⲩⲓⲧ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲛⲁⲗⲟⲅⲓⲥ- ⲙⲟⲥ ⲁⲛ ⲥⲉⲑⲟⲗⲉⲃ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲭⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉϭⲓ ϩⲁⲡ ⲛⲉⲙⲏⲓ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϯⲛⲁⲓ̈ⲛⲓ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲛⲧⲁⲁⲡⲟⲫⲁⲥⲓⲥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϯⲉⲣ ⲟⲙⲟⲗⲟⲅⲓⲛ ϫⲉ ϯⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲫⲙⲟⲩ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣϧⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ϯⲛⲁⲫⲱⲧ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̀ϩⲗⲓ ⲉⲑⲣⲓⲓⲛⲓ ϣⲁⲣⲟⲕ ϯϣⲓⲛⲓ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲧⲛⲁⲏⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧⲕ ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲕⲱϯ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲇ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉⲛⲉϩ ⲉϣⲁϥϯ ⲙⲉⲧⲛⲁⲏⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲉϣⲁϥⲧⲏⲓⲥ ⲛϫⲓⲛϫⲏ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ
30 1. Have pity on me, as good. Heal me, as physician (Ex 15:26). Lead me back, as shepherd who guides. Free me, as powerful. 2. 'Make me live for your mercy' (Ps 6:5b). As Savior and philanthropist 'make me live for your mercy'. 31 1. Not because of my works: in fact they are evil. Not because of my sufferings: in fact they are light. Not because of my words: in fact they are empty. Not because of my thoughts: in fact they are dirty. Rather, 'make me live for your mercy' (Ps 6:5b). 2. In fact, if you want to make a case against me, I myself will formulate my sentence alone. I admit to deserving death; nevertheless, 'make me live for your mercy' (Ps 6:5b). 3. I will find escape in your mercy. I have nothing to bring to you. I seek commiseration from you. Do not ask for justice from me. In fact, I have nothing. No one ever sells commiseration, but gives it for free. For this 'make me live for your mercy' (Ps 6:5b).
ⲫⲁⲓ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲛ ⲛⲉⲕⲥⲁϫⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲕϫⲟⲧⲟⲩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲅⲣⲁⲫⲏ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲉⲣⲉⲡϩⲏⲧ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓ ⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲭⲏ ⲉⲣⲣⲏⲓ ⲛⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ
32 1. Remember your words that you have pronounced in the Holy Scriptures. 2. Remember, Lord, that the heart of man lies always on evil things


[p. 482] {#po.52.p482}
ⲉϫⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲡⲉⲧϩⲱⲟⲩ ⲓⲥϫⲉⲛ ⲧⲉϥⲙⲉⲧⲁⲗⲟⲩ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲑⲁⲙⲓⲟⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲕⲁϩⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲟⲩⲟⲛⲧⲏⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̀ⲣⲣⲏⲓ ⲛ̅ϧⲏⲧ ⲙⲡϣⲁϩ ⲙⲡⲭⲣⲱⲙ ⲙⲡⲥⲛⲟϥ ⲛ̅ϯⲥⲁⲣⲝ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲁⲣⲉⲫⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲧⲉⲛⲑⲱⲛⲧ ⲉⲟⲩⲙⲉⲧ̀ⲉⲫⲗⲏⲟⲩ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ϩⲗⲓ ⲉϥⲟⲛϧ ⲛⲁⲑⲙⲁⲓ̈ⲟ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲙⲑⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲁⲕϣⲁⲛϯ ϩⲑⲏⲕ ⲉⲁⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ϩⲗⲓ ⲛⲁϣⲟϩⲓ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ϩⲗⲓ ⲉϥⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲉⲑⲱⲗⲉⲃ ⲕⲁⲛ ⲟⲩⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲟⲩⲱⲧ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉϥⲱⲛϧ ϩⲓϫⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲕⲁϩⲓ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲉⲧⲁⲩⲉⲣ ⲃⲟⲕⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲁ‐ ⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲁⲧⲁⲙⲁⲩ ϭⲓ ϣⲓϣⲱⲟⲩ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ ⲡϭⲥ̅ ϫⲉ ⲛⲑⲟⲥ ϯⲕⲉⲫⲉ ⲥⲧⲟⲩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲧ ⲁⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲙⲑⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛ̅ⲑⲱⲟⲩ ⲛⲓⲥⲓⲟⲩ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲓⲕⲉⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲥⲉⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲁⲛ ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲁⲟⲩ‐ ⲙⲏϣ ϩⲉⲓ ϫⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲣⲉⲛ ⲧⲫⲉ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲛⲟⲩⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣⲟⲓ
since his youth (Gn 8:21). Remember, Lord, that you created us precisely with (the) earth and that (I) have in me the flame of fire (that is) the blood of the flesh. Remember, Lord, that 'man is like vanity' (Ps 144:4). 3. Remember, Lord, that 'no living being will be justified before you' (Ps 143:2). Remember, Lord, that if you pay attention to iniquity, no one will be able to remain standing (Ps 130:3). Remember, Lord, that there is no one who is pure from stain, even if his life on earth is (of) a single day (Jb 14:4-5). 4. Remember, Lord, that 'I was conceived in iniquity, and in sins my mother conceived me' (Ps 51:7). Remember, Lord, that even heaven is not pure before you, nor the stars themselves and not even the angels; they are not pure, if many have fallen from heaven because of their sins (Gn 6:1-4). 5. For this 'make me live, Lord, for your mercy' (Ps 6:5b), (precisely I) who have need of mercy.
ⲡϭⲥ̅ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲣⲁ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲣ ⲭⲣⲓⲁ ⲙⲡⲓⲛⲁⲓ ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲕⲧⲟⲩϫⲟ ⲙⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲫⲏⲣⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲧⲉ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲭⲛⲁⲓ ⲙⲡⲓⲑⲙⲏⲓ ⲛⲟⲩϣⲫⲏⲣⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲧⲉ ϥⲉⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲕϯ ⲱⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲥⲡⲟⲩⲇⲉⲟⲥ ⲟⲩⲛⲓϣϯ ⲁⲛ ⲡⲉ ⲟⲩⲇⲓⲕⲉⲟⲛ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲙⲁⲗⲗⲟⲛ ⲙⲁⲣⲟⲩⲉⲣ ϣⲫⲏⲣⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲉϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ϧⲁ ⲡⲓⲁⲧⲉⲙⲡϣⲁ ϫⲉ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲥϭⲓ ⲱⲟⲩ ⲛϫⲉ ⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲛ̀ⲣⲣⲏⲓ ⲛϧⲏⲧ ⲉⲡⲓⲇⲏ ⲡⲓⲥⲏⲓⲛⲓ ⲛⲥⲱⲧⲡ ⲛⲉϣⲁⲩⲧⲁⲓⲟϥ ⲁⲛ ⲛϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛⲥⲉⲉⲣ ϣⲫⲏⲣⲓ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲁ ⲑⲣⲉϥⲧⲁⲗϭⲟ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲁⲧⲧⲁⲗϭⲟ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲛⲥⲉⲉⲣ ϩⲉⲗⲡⲓⲥ ⲉⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲡⲧⲣⲏϥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲡⲓⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲛ̀ⲁⲅⲁⲑⲟⲥ ⲛⲉϣⲁⲩϯ ⲱⲟⲩ ⲛⲁϥ ⲁⲛ ⲛ̅ ϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛ̀ⲥⲉⲉⲣ ⲉⲩⲫⲏⲙⲓⲛ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲁ ⲑⲣⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲭⲁⲣⲓⲍⲉⲥⲑⲉ ⲙⲡⲱⲛϧ ⲛⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲙⲫⲙⲟⲩ
33 1. Indeed, if you save someone who is worthy of it, it is not a wonderful thing. And if you have pity on the just, it is not a wonderful thing; indeed, he is worthy of it. If you glorify the zealous, it is not a great thing; this, in fact, is what is just. 2. Rather, act so that your mercy toward me, me, the unworthy one, may be admired, so that your philanthropy may be glorified in me. 3. For the excellent physician is not respected before, and is not admired (before), but after having healed the incurables for whom there is no hope. And the good king is not glorified before, nor (before) is he praised, but after having granted life by virtue of his philanthropy to those who deserve death.
ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉϥⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲕⲥⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲛϯⲙⲉⲧϫⲱⲃ ⲛ̇ⲧⲉ ϯⲫⲩⲥⲓⲥ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲑⲁⲙⲓⲟⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲕⲥⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϩⲱⲥ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲧⲏⲓⲥ ϩⲓⲱⲧⲕ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲕⲧⲟⲩϫⲟⲥ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϫⲉ ϯⲛⲟⲩ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛϧⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ
34 1. You know the weakness of the nature that you created, and you know it because you took it upon yourself and saved it. 2. For this reason, now, "make me live by your mercy" (Ps 6:50).


[p. 484] {#po.52.p484}
ⲙⲡⲉⲛⲑⲣⲉⲥϭⲣⲟ ⲛϫⲉ ⲧⲁⲕⲁⲕⲓⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧ̀ϣⲁⲛϩⲑⲏϥ ⲙⲡⲉⲛ‐ ⲑⲣⲉⲥϭⲣⲟ ⲛϫⲉ ⲧⲁⲙⲉⲧⲁⲙⲉⲗⲉⲥ ⲉⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲛⲁⲧϭⲣⲟ ⲉⲣⲟⲥ ⲁⲕϣⲁⲛⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉϯ ϩⲁⲡ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ⲣⲱϥ ⲛⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲑⲱⲙ ⲉⲉϩⲙⲡⲁⲩϫⲉⲙ ϩⲗⲓ ⲛⲥⲁϫⲓ ⲉϫⲱ
3. May my wickedness not be victorious over your compassion. May my negligence not be victorious over your invincible philanthropy. 4. Indeed, if you wish to judge us, the mouth of any man will be closed without having found any word to say.
ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲁϣⲁⲓ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲛⲉⲕⲡⲉⲑⲛⲁⲛⲉⲩ ⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲓⲉ ⲛⲧⲉⲛⲉⲣ ⲟⲩⲱ ⲛⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧⲁⲅⲁⲑⲟⲥ ⲁⲕⲑⲁⲙⲓⲟⲛ ⲉⲛϣⲟⲡ ⲁⲛ ⲁⲕⲑⲣⲉⲛϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲁⲕⲧⲁⲛϣⲟⲛ ⲁⲕⲉⲣ ϧⲏⲓⲃⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲛ ⲁⲕⲑⲣⲉⲛⲁⲓⲁⲓ ⲁⲕⲑⲉⲃⲓⲟ ⲛⲉⲛⲭⲁⲓ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲥⲁⲡⲉⲥⲏⲧ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲛ ϭⲁⲗⲁⲩϫ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲱⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡϫⲓⲛⲑⲣⲉⲛⲥⲱⲣⲉⲙ ⲁⲕⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟⲛ ⲁⲕϣⲟⲡⲧⲉⲛ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕⲥⲛⲟϥ ⲙⲙⲓⲛ ⲙⲙⲟⲕ ⲉⲛⲥⲟⲣⲉⲙ ⲁⲕⲕⲱϯ ⲛⲥⲱⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲕϫⲉⲙⲧⲉⲛ ⲁⲕϥⲁⲓ ⲣⲁⲣⲟⲛ ⲉⲛ ⲃⲏϣ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲕϩⲟⲡⲥⲧⲉⲛ ⲉⲛⲟⲓ ⲛϩⲏⲕⲓ ⲁⲕⲁⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲛ̅ ⲣⲁⲙⲁⲟ̅ ⲁⲕⲁⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲛϣⲏⲣⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲥⲟⲛ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲕⲗⲏⲣⲟ‐ ⲛⲟⲙⲟⲥ ⲙ̅Ⲫϯ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛϣⲫⲏⲣ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲗⲏⲣⲟⲛⲟⲙⲟⲥ ⲛⲉⲙⲁⲕ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲛⲟⲛ ⲧⲉⲛⲛⲁϫⲉⲙ ϩⲗⲓ ⲛ̀ⲁⲡⲟⲗⲟⲅⲓⲁ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲑⲣⲉⲛϫⲱ
35 1. Indeed, because of the multitude of your benefits, we will not be able to confess or narrate your goodness. 2. You created us from non-being (2 Mac 7:28). You made us be (Wis 1:14). You multiplied us. You protected us. You made us increase (Gn 1:26-28). You lowered everything under our feet (Ps 8:7). Then, when we were lost, you made us return. You redeemed us with your own blood (Rv 5:9). Lost, you went in search of us, you found us and you supported us. Stripped, you covered us. Poor, you made us rich, you made us son, brother, heir of God, your co-heir (Gal 4:7). 3. And so we will not even find an excuse to say about these things.
ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲣⲓ ⲉϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲉϭⲓ ϩⲁⲡ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲉⲕⲉⲃⲓⲁⲓⲕ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲣϣⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲛⲁⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲉⲕⲁⲡⲩⲗⲏ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕϩⲟ ⲥⲁⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲛ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲣϣⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲣⲁⲕⲥ ⲫⲟⲛϫⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲣⲁⲕⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲫⲁⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉⲛϩⲉⲧⲫⲱ ⲉⲧϩⲟⲣϣ ⲉⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲟⲃϣⲕ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲕⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲉϩⲛⲁⲕ ⲉⲡϩⲣⲟϣ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲛ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲡⲁϣⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲛⲁⲛ ⲟ‐
36 1. For this reason "do not enter into judgment with your servants" (Ps 143:2). Do not measure our iniquities with your threats, but turn your face (away) from our sins (Ps 51:11). Do not measure them precisely on a scale, but tilt it with philanthropy, overturn it and tilt from this to that (Ps 75:9), from our heavy burden to your mercy. With all your will, forget the weight of our sins and the multitude of our iniquities beyond measure.
ⲙⲓⲁ ⲉⲧⲟⲓ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲉⲡⲓϣⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛϧⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧϥ ⲉⲧⲁⲩⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ϫⲉ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲁⲩⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲕⲉ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲁⲓϣⲁⲛϫⲟⲥ ⲉⲙⲱⲩⲥⲏⲥ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲛⲑⲟϥ ⲟⲩⲁⲧⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲡⲉ ⲕⲁⲧⲁ ⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲉⲧⲁⲥⲉⲣ ⲙⲉⲑⲣⲉ ⲛϫⲉ ϯⲅⲣⲁⲫⲏ “ ⲕⲁⲛ ⲁⲁⲣⲱⲛ ⲕⲁⲛ ⲇⲁⲩⲓⲇ ϯⲉⲙⲓ ϫⲉ ⲕⲁⲛ ⲛ̅ⲑⲟϥ ⲡⲉⲧⲣⲟⲥ ⲡⲓⲕⲟⲣⲓⲫⲉⲟⲥ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲡⲓⲛⲓϣϯ ⲛⲁⲡⲟⲥⲧⲟⲗⲟⲥ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲛⲑⲟϥ ⲉⲛⲟⲩⲁⲧⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲡⲉ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛ̅ ϥⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲁⲛ
37 1. "Make me live by your mercy," that by means of which all the saved have been saved. 2. Indeed, if I speak of Moses, (I will say that) even he is not without sin, as Scripture testifies (Ex 2:11-12); nor Aaron (Ex 32:1-6), nor David (1 Sam 11). I know that not even Peter himself (Mt 26:70), that coryphaeus and great apostle, not even he is without sin and is not pure.


[p. 486] {#po.52.p486}
ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲛⲁϩϯ ϯⲕⲱϯ ⲛⲥⲁ ⲟⲩⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓ ⲛⲁϩϯ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲟⲩⲱϩ ⲥⲁϩⲛⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟϥ ϫⲉⲕⲁⲥ ⲛⲧⲉⲕϫⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲓ ϩⲱⲥ ⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ϫⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁϩϯ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲛⲁϩⲙⲉⲕ ⲙⲁϣⲉ ⲛⲁⲕ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩϩⲓⲣⲏⲛⲏ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲁⲕⲭⲱ ⲛⲱⲟⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲡⲁϭⲥ̅ ⲙⲡⲉⲕϫⲟⲥ ⲛ̀ϩⲗⲓ ϫⲉ ⲙⲁϣⲉ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲛⲉⲕϩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲑⲛⲁⲛⲉⲩ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲁⲩⲛⲁϩⲙⲉⲕ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ϫⲉ ⲉⲣⲉⲧⲉⲛⲙⲉⲑⲙⲏⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲥ ⲟⲓ ⲙⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲛϯⲧⲱⲓⲥⲓ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲑⲏ ⲉⲧⲟⲓ ⲛϣⲣⲱ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲙⲑⲟ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ
38 1. For this reason, with faith I seek salvation, with the faith that you commanded, so that you, as a philanthropist, may say to me regarding these things: "It is your faith that has saved you. Go in peace!" (Mk 5:34//). 2. (To) those whom you have forgiven, my Lord, you did not say at all: "It is your good works that have saved you," since all our justice is like the cloth of one who has her menstruation before you (Is 64:5). 3. For this reason, I say: "Make me live by your mercy" (Ps 6:50).
ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϯϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲁϭⲟϫⲓ ⲛ̀ⲥⲱⲓ ⲛ̀ⲛⲓⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲁⲱⲛϧ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲁϭⲟϫⲓ ⲛⲥⲱⲓ ⲛⲛⲓⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉϥⲑⲣⲉⲡⲓⲡⲉⲧϩⲱⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲉⲓ ⲥⲁⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ⲡⲓⲣⲉϥϭⲟϫⲓ ϥⲓⲏⲥ ⲛⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲉϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲡⲓ‐ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲁϭⲟϫⲓ ⲛⲥⲱⲓ ⲛⲧⲉϥⲁⲙⲟⲛⲓ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉϥⲉⲛⲧ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲭⲁⲗⲓⲛⲟⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲕⲩⲙⲟⲥ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲣⲁ ⲡⲉⲧϧⲉⲛⲧ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲁⲛ ⲛ̅ ⲑⲟⲕ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ Ⲫϯ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲓⲣⲓ ⲛⲛⲓϣⲫⲏⲣⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲙⲁⲣⲟⲩⲉⲣ ϣⲫⲏⲣⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ⲉϫⲱⲛ ⲫⲏ
39 1. And your mercy will pursue me in the days of my life (Ps 23:6). Your mercy will pursue me all the days and will distance evil from me. The runner always hastens toward sin. Your mercy will pursue me and will restrain me, will carry me with a bridle and a muzzle, (precisely) I who am not close to you (Ps 32:9). 2. Indeed, you are the only God who performs wonders (Ps 136:4). 3. For this reason, may your mercy be admired toward us, O (you) who save those who have hope in you (Ps 17:7).
ⲉⲧⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ⲛ̅ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲣ ϩⲉⲗⲡⲓⲥ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲛⲑⲟⲕ ⲡⲉ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ⲣⲓ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲉⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲛϩⲉⲣ ϩⲉⲗⲡⲓⲥ ⲉⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲁϥϯ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲟⲩⲟⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲡⲁϭⲥ̅ ⲛϫⲉ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲣ ⲭⲣⲉⲱⲥⲧⲓⲛ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲛⲟⲩⲑⲃⲁ ⲛ̅ϫⲓⲛϭⲱⲣ ⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲉⲧⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲕ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲉⲕⲟⲩϫⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϩⲱⲥ ⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲁⲕϯ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϫⲱⲕ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲁϥ ⲛⲟⲩⲑⲃⲁ ⲛϫⲓⲛϭⲱⲣ Ϫ ⲙⲏ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲁ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲙⲉⲥⲧⲉ ⲡⲉϥⲥⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲁⲕⲱⲥ Ⲙⲙⲡⲉϥⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟϥ ϣⲁⲣⲟⲕ ⲛϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲉϥϯ ϩⲟ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲙⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϣⲏⲣⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲧⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲉⲁϥⲁⲓϥ ⲙⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉⲕⲣⲉⲙⲃⲉⲭⲉ
40 1. You are the one whose mercy does more than we expect. 2. He went toward you, my Lord, he who owed you ten thousand talents and asked you for a little more time; and (you), as a philanthropist, completely remitted the ten thousand talents to him; if after, indeed, he had not at least hated his brother so wickedly (Mt 18:24). 3. Did he not return to you before, imploring you like the prodigal son, having made himself like your hired servants?


[p. 488] {#po.52.p488}
ⲛ̅ ⲑⲟⲕ ⲇⲉ ⲁⲕⲁⲓϥ ⲛϣⲏⲣⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲗⲏⲣⲟⲛⲟⲙⲟⲥ ⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲉⲧⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲕ ⲛϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲥⲟⲛⲓ ⲉϭⲓ ⲛⲟⲩⲉⲣ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ⲑⲟⲕ ⲇⲉ ϧⲉⲛ ϯⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩ ⲁⲕⲉⲣ ⲭⲁⲣⲓⲍⲉⲥⲑⲉ ⲛⲁϥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓ ⲡⲁⲣⲁⲇⲓⲥⲟⲥ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲁⲥϯ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲥⲟⲩⲟⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲛϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲡⲟⲣⲛⲏ ⲉⲥⲣⲓⲙⲓ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧⲥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲥⲉⲣ ⲉⲧⲓⲛ ⲇⲉ ⲛ̀ϩⲗⲓ ⲁⲥϭⲓ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲉⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲁⲥⲉⲣ ϩⲉⲗⲡⲓⲥ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲛⲉⲙ ϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲉⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲥⲕⲱϯ ⲛⲥⲱϥ ⲁϥⲣⲓⲙⲓ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲉⲧⲣⲟⲥ ⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲉⲧⲓⲛ ⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧⲕ ⲛ̅ⲑⲟⲕ ⲇⲉ ⲁⲕϯ ⲛⲁϥ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓϣⲟϣⲧ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ϯⲉⲕⲕⲗⲏⲥⲓⲁ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ
You made him an heir son (Lk 15:19). 4. A thief asked you to carry (his) only remembrance in your kingdom; but you, instantly, gifted him the entire paradise (Lk 23:40-43). 5. A prostitute approached you only by weeping and, therefore, asked for nothing; (yet) she received more than she expected and more than she sought (Lk 7:37-48). 6. Peter wept (Mt 26:75), asked you for forgiveness, and yet you gave him the keys of the Church (and) of the Kingdom of heaven (Mt 16:19).
ⲑⲙⲉⲧⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲫⲏⲟⲩⲓ· ⲟⲩⲡⲁⲓⲣⲏϯ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲑⲁⲙⲓⲟ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲁⲩⲓⲁ ⲧⲟⲧⲟⲩ ⲛⲥⲱⲟⲩ ⲓⲥϫⲉⲛ ϣⲟⲣⲡ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲱⲟⲩ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ϩⲗⲓ ⲛϩⲉⲗⲡⲓⲥ ⲛⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ϧⲏⲧⲕ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲁⲩⲫⲟϩ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲡⲕⲁϩⲓ ⲛ̅ⲁⲙⲉⲛϯ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲫⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲉⲕⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲛⲱⲟⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ ⲑⲙⲉⲧⲛⲓϣϯ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲟⲛ ϯϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϯⲛⲁⲕⲏⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲓϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ
41 1. Your creation is like this: to those who have despaired (from) the beginning, to those who have no hope of salvation in you, to those who have arrived alone at the land of Hades following sin, (it is) to them (that) you manifest the greatness of your philanthropy. 2. For this reason I say it again and I will not stop saying it: "Make me live by your mercy" (Ps 6:50).

### On Ps 6:6
ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ ϫⲉ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧϧⲉⲛ ⲫⲙⲟⲩ ⲉϥⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉⲑⲛⲁ‐ ϣⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲁⲙⲉⲛϯ: ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϯⲓⲏⲥ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϯⲥⲉⲃⲧⲱⲧ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϯⲣϧⲟⲥⲓ ⲉⲓⲉ̀ⲙⲓ ϫⲉ ⲁϥⲣⲱⲛⲧ ⲉⲣⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲣⲟⲓ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡϫⲱⲕ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲱⲛⲣϧ ϯⲛⲁⲉⲣ ⲉⲝⲟⲙⲟ‐ ⲗⲟⲅⲓⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲙ̅Ⲫϯ ⲉⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲁⲙⲉⲛϯ'' ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉⲡⲧⲏⲣϥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲁⲙⲉⲛϯ'2 ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲁ ⲫⲙⲟⲩ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ϫⲓⲛⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙϩⲁⲩ ⲟⲩϣⲁⲓ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲁⲃⲓⲟⲥ ⲫⲱⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲫⲟⲥⲟⲛ ⲁⲡⲓⲥⲇⲁⲧⲓⲟⲛ'5 ⲟⲩⲱⲧⲉⲃ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ϩⲗⲓ ⲛⲁϣⲉⲣ ϣⲱⲓϫ'
42 1. "For there is no one in death who remembers you. Who will be able to confess you in Hades?" (Ps 6:5) 2. For this reason I hasten, for this reason I prepare myself, for this reason I suffer, knowing that the end of my life is approaching. He will not confess God at all in Hades (Sir 17:22). 3. In Hades, indeed, there is no repentance. There is no forgiveness after death. There is no remission in the tomb. 4. It is a festival, indeed, my life. And every time the stadium closes, no one


[p. 490] {#po.52.p490}
ⲛⲧⲉϥ ϭⲓ ⲭⲗⲟⲙ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉⲫⲟⲥⲟⲛ ⲁϥⲧⲁϩⲟⲓ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲉϫⲱⲣϩ ⲙⲡⲁⲧⲉⲥⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛϫⲉ ϯⲁⲅⲟⲣⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓϩⲏⲟⲩ”
will be able to compete and will be able to receive the crown in that place. Every time the night has surprised me, there has been no market nor profit.
ⲥⲱⲧⲉⲙ ϫⲉ ⲟⲩ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲉϯⲅⲣⲁⲫⲏ ϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ “ⲉⲧⲁϥϫⲉⲕ ⲡⲉϥⲃⲓⲟⲥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲁⲗⲱⲥ'' ϫⲉ ⲫⲙⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲙⲧⲟⲛ ⲙⲡⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲁϥϩⲟⲡϥ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲙⲱⲓⲧ ⲁⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ϣⲑⲁⲙ ⲉⲣⲱϥ ⲣⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲁ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲉⲧⲁⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ϣⲑⲁⲙ ⲉⲣⲱϥ ⲁϣ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲡⲉ ⲑⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ⲙⲡⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲛⲣⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ
43 1. Listen to what Scripture says regarding every man who has completed his life well: "Death is a rest for man, the way (of it) is hidden. The Lord has closed his door." (Job 3:23) 2. Indeed, in the place of which the Lord has closed the door, what is the repentance of the sinner in that place?
ⲡⲁⲓⲃⲓⲟⲥ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲫⲁ ⲛⲓⲣⲓⲥⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲫⲁ ⲛⲓⲭⲗⲟⲙ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲃⲓⲟⲥ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲫⲁ ⲑⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲙ̅Ⲫϯ ⲡⲉ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲇⲉ ⲫⲁ ϯϫⲓⲛϯ ϩⲁⲡ ⲙⲙⲏⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲙⲁⲧⲁⲛⲣⲟⲓ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲉⲕⲛⲁⲓ̈ ϫⲉ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲫⲏ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲛ ⲫⲙⲟⲩ ⲉϥⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲛⲁⲕ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲁⲙⲉⲛϯ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲡⲉ: ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ϩⲗⲓ
3. This life is that of sufferings, that of there is that of crowns (Jas 1:12); this life is that of the philanthropy of God, that of there is, instead, that of the truthful judgment (Jn 5:30). 4. For this reason, "make me live by your mercy, because there is no one in death who remembers you. Who will be able to confess you in Hades?" (Ps 6:5b-6). Who? No one!
ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲛⲁⲛⲉⲥ ⲉⲉⲣ ϣⲟⲣⲡ “ ⲛⲥⲉⲃⲧⲱⲧⲕ ⲉⲡⲓⲙⲱⲓⲧ ⲙⲙⲟϣⲓ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲟⲩϭⲓ ⲙⲱⲓⲧ ϧⲁϫⲱⲛ ⲛ̀ⲣⲏⲧϥ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛ ⲕⲟⲧⲥ ϫⲉⲕⲁⲥ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲉϥⲧⲁϩⲉ ⲧⲉⲛⲁ̀ⲡⲟⲣⲓⲁ ϩⲱⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲉⲧϩⲏⲕⲓ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲭⲁⲕⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲉϫⲱⲣϩ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲛⲓⲡⲁⲣⲑⲉⲛⲟⲥ ⲛ̅ⲥⲟϫ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲉϥⲥⲱⲟⲩⲛ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲕⲉⲥⲟⲫⲟⲥ ⲉϥⲧⲟⲩⲛⲟⲥ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉϥⲛⲉϩⲥⲓ ⲛⲧⲉⲛⲙⲉⲧⲁⲙⲉⲗⲉⲥ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲥⲉⲃⲧⲉ ⲛⲉⲕϩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲧⲉⲕϫⲓⲛⲓ̈ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲣⲱⲟⲩϣ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲕⲕⲟⲓ ϫⲉ ⲉⲣⲉⲛⲓⲉϩⲟⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲫⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲟⲓ ⲙ̅ⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲛⲟⲩⲥⲱⲟⲩⲃⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲣⲉⲡⲱⲟⲩ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲙ̅ⲫⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲟⲓ ⲙ̅ⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲛⲟⲩϩⲣⲏⲣⲓ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲟⲩⲥⲱⲟⲩⲃⲉⲛ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲓⲣⲏϯ ⲉⲧⲉϥⲛⲁⲫⲓⲣⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛⲧⲉϥϣⲱⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛ̀ⲧⲉⲥ‐ ϥⲟⲣϥⲉⲣ ⲛϫⲉ ⲧⲉϥϩⲣⲏⲣⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲡⲥⲁⲓ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲡⲉϥϩⲟ ⲁϥⲧⲁⲕⲟ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϫⲉ ⲥⲁⲣⲝ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲧⲥⲙⲏ ⲛ̅ ⲟⲩϭⲁϫ ⲉⲧⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲡⲉ
44 1. For this reason it is beautiful to prepare in advance for the itinerary in that place, thanks to which we are sufficiently guided so that our own anguish of poverty is not surprised by the darkness and by that night of the foolish virgins (Mt 25:1-13), 2. that which even the wise man knows, when, lifting us up and waking our negligence, he says: "Prepare your things for your departure and take care of your field" (Prv 24:27) because the days of man are like grass (Ps 103:15) and "all the glory of man is like the flower of the grass" (Is 40:6). Thus it will bloom and then it will dry up and its flower will fall, and the beauty of its face is ruined (Jas 1:10-11). 3. And every flesh will rise at the sound of a sparrow (Eccl 12:4), that is, at the sound


[p. 492] {#po.52.p492}
ϫⲉ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲥⲙⲏ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲁⲣⲭⲏⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲥⲁⲣⲝ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ϯⲣϧⲁⲓ̈ⲏ ⲛ̅ ⲥⲁⲗⲡⲓⲅⲝ ⲥⲉⲃⲧⲉ ⲛⲉⲕϩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲉⲡⲉⲕⲙⲱⲓⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲣⲱⲟⲩϣ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲕⲕⲟⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲟⲛ ϥϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲕⲣⲁⲓⲏⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲭⲛⲁⲉⲣ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲛⲉϩ
of the archangel every flesh will rise from the dead at the (sound of the) final trumpet (1 Th 4:16). 4. "Prepare your things for your departure. Take care of your field." (Prv 24:27); and again he says: Remember your end, and you will never sin (Sir 7:36).
ⲉϥⲓⲣⲓ ⲙ̅ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲓ ⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲓⲣⲁⲓⲏ ⲛ̅ⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲛ̀ⲧⲁϥ ⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲛ̅ϯϩⲟϯ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲉⲙ ϣⲁ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲟⲩⲛⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉⲧⲟⲓ ⲛϩⲟϯ ⲉϥϭⲓ ⲙⲱⲓⲧ ⲣⲁϫⲱⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩϩⲏⲃⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲣⲓⲙⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲉϫⲱⲣϩ ⲛϣⲣⲱⲓⲥ
45 1. The prophet, remembering at every moment such an end and being constantly and forever afraid of that tremendous instant, guides us into compunction, into affliction, into weeping and into nightly vigil.

### On Ps 6:7
ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϥⲓⲛⲓ ⲙⲙⲟϥ ⲉϫⲱⲛ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲁⲓⲣⲓⲥⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲁϥⲓ ⲁϩⲟⲙ ϯⲛⲁϫⲱⲕⲉⲙ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁϭⲗⲟϫ ⲛⲉϫⲱⲣϩ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲉⲣⲙ‐ ⲱⲟⲩⲓ ϯⲛⲁϩⲱⲣⲡ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲫⲣⲏϣ ⲉϥⲥⲱⲟⲩⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲧⲁϫⲣⲟ ϫⲉ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧϭⲟ ϩⲓϫⲉⲛ ⲡⲕⲁϩⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲁⲓⲙⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲧⲉⲗⲧⲓⲗⲓ ⲛ̀ⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲃⲁⲗ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲱⲥⲣ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲑⲉⲗⲏⲗ ⲛ̅ϯⲙⲉⲧⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲛⲓⲫⲏⲟⲩⲓ ⲉⲑⲙⲏⲛ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ “ ϥϫⲱ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲱⲟⲩ ⲛⲓⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲧⲉⲣ ϩⲏⲃⲓ ϯⲛⲟⲩ ϫⲉ ⲛ ⲑⲱⲟⲩ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲟⲩⲛⲁϯ ϩⲟ ⲉⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲟϣⲓ ⲁⲩⲙⲟϣⲓ ϩⲓ ⲫⲙⲱⲓⲧ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲓⲉⲛⲧⲟⲗⲏ ⲁⲩⲣⲓⲙⲓ ⲉⲩⲥⲓϯ ⲛ ⲛⲟⲩϭⲣⲱϫ ⲉⲩⲛⲏⲟⲩ ⲇⲉ ⲥⲉⲛⲁⲉ̀ⲛⲟⲩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲑⲉⲗⲏⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ϯⲁⲛⲁⲥⲧⲁⲥⲓⲥ
46 1. For this reason he applies it to us saying: "I am exhausted in my sighing, I will wash my bed every night, with my tears I will drench my couch" (Ps 6:6-7) knowing with certainty that those who with (the) drops of tears from their eyes sow on the earth, in this place, will reap with joy (Ps 126:5) in the kingdom of heaven that remains forever. 2. Indeed, he says: "Blessed are those who are afflicted now, because they will be the ones to be comforted." (Mt 5:4) 3. For this reason, going forward they walked on the way of the commandments, they wept, sowing their seeds. Coming, instead, they will arrive with joy in the resurrection, carrying their sheaves (Ps 126:6).
ⲉⲩϥⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲟⲩⲭⲛⲁⲩ ⲕⲉ ⲅⲁⲣ Ⲙⲫⲣⲏϯ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲱⲟⲩ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲡ̅ⲛ̅ⲁ̅ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲣⲏϯ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲫⲙⲱⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲭⲣⲱⲙ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϥⲓ ⲁϩⲟⲙ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓ ⲙⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲧⲉⲛⲛⲁϭⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲱⲙⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲧⲟⲩⲃⲟ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲛⲥⲩⲙⲙⲉⲇⲟⲭⲟⲥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓ ⲡⲛ̅ⲁ̅ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲩϯ ⲙⲡⲓⲱⲙⲥ ⲉⲛⲉϩ
47 1. As in water and in Spirit, indeed, so also by means of the water of tears, of the fire of sighing and of compunction we will be washed and purified and will become participants of the Holy Spirit. 2. Without the operation


[p. 494] {#po.52.p494}
ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲡⲓⲱⲙⲥ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲡⲓⲙⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲭⲱⲣⲓⲥ ⲧⲉⲛⲉⲣⲅⲓⲁ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲡⲛⲉⲩⲙⲁ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲛⲥⲉϯ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲁⲛ. ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲙⲡⲉⲣⲉⲣ ϣⲫⲏⲣⲓ ⲡⲟⲗⲗⲁⲕⲓⲥ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛⲥⲉϫⲓⲙⲓ ⲙⲡⲓϩⲙⲟⲧ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲙ̅ⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ⲛ̅ⲣϧⲏⲧⲉⲛ ⲉϥⲟⲓ ⲛⲁⲛⲁⲅⲕⲉⲟⲛ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲧ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲡⲓⲱⲙⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯϫⲓⲛⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲑⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲣⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲱⲙⲥ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲉⲧⲟⲩϯ ⲱⲙⲥ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲛ̀ⲣⲏⲧϥ ⲉⲛⲟⲓ ⲛ̅ⲕⲟⲩϫⲓ ⲓ̈ⲧⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡϫⲓⲛ‐ ⲑⲣⲉⲛⲁⲓⲁⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲁⲛⲑⲱⲗⲉⲃ. ⲁⲛϩⲉⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲓϫⲓⲛⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲓⲥⲙⲟⲧ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲇⲉ ⲟⲛ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ϣϫⲟⲙ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ⲉϭⲓ ⲱⲙⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϥⲧⲟⲩⲃⲟ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ⲙⲙⲏⲛⲓ ϣⲁ ⲧⲉⲛⲙⲉⲧϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϣⲁ ⲡⲉⲛⲣⲁⲉ ⲛⲛⲓϥⲓ ⲕⲁⲧⲁ ⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲉⲧⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲙⲉⲑⲣⲉ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧϣⲟⲡ
of the Holy Spirit, neither baptism, nor compunction, nor tears are ever given. 3. Do not be surprised! Often, indeed, by virtue of baptism, the grace of the much-needed compunction like the remission that is in holy baptism come to be found within us when, as little ones, we are baptized. Then, during our growth in sin, we dirtied ourselves. 4. In this manner we found such remission, but thanks to tears we can still receive baptism and, as what is found in the Psalms testifies, this (baptism) purifies us every day until our old age and until our last breath. On Ps 6:8
ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲯⲁⲗⲙⲟⲥ. ϥϫⲱ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲁϥⲑⲟϩ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲁⲃⲁⲗ ⲛⲧⲉⲛ ⲡϫⲱⲛⲧ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲁⲡⲁⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲁⲓⲱⲥⲕ. ⲉⲧⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲡⲉ ϫⲉ ⲁⲩⲧⲁⲟⲩⲟⲓ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ⲁⲓⲁⲓⲁⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲁⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲡⲓⲡⲉⲧϩⲱⲟⲩ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲡⲉ ⲛⲓⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟϥ ϫⲉ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲁⲡⲁⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ.
48 1. Indeed, he says: "My eye has been agitated by anger" (Ps 6:7a) and by the thought of my soul; "I have grown old among all my enemies" (Ps 6:7b) I have become old. 2. That is, I have been cast down, I have grown and I have aged together with my sins. All these sins are very bad. This is, indeed, what he says: "I have grown old among all my enemies" (Ps 6:7b).

### Ps 6:8-10
ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲥⲟⲙⲥ ϫⲉ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲁ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲡⲁⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲥⲏⲟⲩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲙⲉⲛⲉⲛⲥⲁ ⲡⲓϫⲓⲛⲱⲛⲣ ϧⲉⲛ ⲁⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲓⲁ ⲧⲟⲧϥ ⲛ̅ⲥⲱϥ ⲙ̅ⲙⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟϥ ⲛϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲁⲧϩⲉⲗⲡⲓⲥ ⲉⲡⲓⲛⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⪓ϯ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥⲧⲏⲓϥ ⲙ̅ⲙⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟϥ ⲉϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲙⲉⲗⲉⲥ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲁϥⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙ̅ⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲁϥⲧⲟⲩⲃⲟ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲁϥⲑⲙⲁⲓ̈ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ϯⲉⲝⲟⲙⲟⲗⲟⲅⲓⲥⲓⲥ. ⲛⲁⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲧⲉⲛⲛⲁϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲛⲁⲓⲧⲟⲩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉⲛⲙⲉⲧϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲉⲛⲣⲁϣⲉ ⲛ̅ⲥⲭⲓⲙ.
49 1. But, look, after the time of aging in sins, after having lived in every iniquity, the same prophet did not despair, did not remain without hope in the mercy of God, did not abandon himself to negligence, but saved himself thanks to compunction, purified himself by means of tears, justified himself by means of confession. 2. All these things we will be able to do (also) during our old age and our last gray hairs.


[p. 496] {#po.52.p496}
ⲉⲓϫⲱ ⲟⲩⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲣⲓⲙⲓ ϥⲓ ⲁϩⲟⲙ ⲙⲁϯ ϩⲟ ⲧⲱⲃϩ. ⲟⲩ ϫⲉ ⲡⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲓ; ⲥⲱⲧⲉⲙ ϥϫⲱ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲁⲡⲁⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ. Ⲓⲧⲁ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲙ̅ⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲉⲥⲑⲁⲛⲉⲥⲑⲉ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ϯϫⲓⲛϣⲱⲡ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲛ̅ⲟⲩⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯϫⲓⲛⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲡⲁⲣⲣⲏⲥⲓⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯϫⲓⲛⲉⲣ ⲟⲩⲱⲓⲛⲓ ⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲉⲡⲓⲧⲓⲙⲁⲛ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲉⲝⲟⲩⲥⲓⲁ ⲛ̅ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲣ ⲡⲟⲗⲉⲙⲓⲛ
50 1. For this reason I say: "Weep, sigh, invoke and supplicate." 2. Then what will happen to us following these things? Listen. Indeed, he says: "I have grown old among all my enemies" (Ps 6:7b). 3. Then, through repentance and holy compunction, and realizing the mercy received of the remission, of the freedom of speech and of the illumination, with authority he rebuked those who fight against him saying:
ⲛⲉⲙⲁϥ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ϩⲉⲛ ⲑⲏ ⲛⲟⲩ ⲥⲁⲃⲟⲗ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲓ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲉⲧⲉⲣ ϩⲱⲃ ⲉϯⲁⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ⲕⲏⲛ ⲉⲣⲱⲧⲉⲛ ϭⲓ ϣⲓⲡⲓ ⲛⲱⲧⲉⲛ ⲁⲣⲓ ⲁⲛⲁⲭⲱⲣⲓⲛ ⲛⲱⲧⲉⲛ ⲥⲁⲃⲟⲗ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲓ ϫⲉ ⲁⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲥⲱⲧⲉⲙ ⲉⲡⲣⲓⲙⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲁⲣⲓⲙⲓ ⲁⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲥⲱⲧⲉⲙ ⲉⲡⲁϯ ϩⲟ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲁⲙⲉⲧϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲁⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ϣⲱⲡ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲡⲣⲟⲥⲉⲩⲭⲏ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲉⲩⲉϭⲓ ϣⲓⲡⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲩⲉϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ ⲛϫⲉ ⲛⲁϫⲁϫⲓ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲉⲩⲉ̀ⲕⲟⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲫⲁϩⲟⲩ ⲉⲩⲉϭⲓ ϣⲓⲡⲓ ⲉⲙⲁϣⲱ ⲛ̅ⲭⲱⲗⲉⲙ.
4. "Depart from me, all you who work iniquity" (Ps 6:8a). Stop it. Be ashamed. Depart from me, "because the Lord has heard the voice of my weeping. The Lord has heard my invocation" (Ps 6:8b-9a) in my old age. "The Lord has accepted my prayer toward Him. Let all my enemies be ashamed and troubled, let them turn back. Let them be ashamed immediately, very much!" (Ps 6:10-11).

### Exhortatory section
Commentary on tears and compunction.

### Do not find excuses for sins
ⲁⲕⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲧϫⲟⲙ ⲛ̅ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲁⲕⲛⲁⲩ ⲉϯⲉⲝⲟ ⲁⲕⲛⲁⲩ ⲉϯϫⲓⲛⲟϩⲓ ⲉⲣⲁⲧⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯϫⲓⲛⲉⲣ ⲁⲡⲁⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉⲣϫⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲓ ϫⲉ ⲁⲛⲟⲕ ⲟⲩϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲛⲁⲧϫⲟⲙ ⲉⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲁⲡⲁⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲛⲓⲃⲉⲛ ϯⲛⲁϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲁⲛ ⲓⲥϫⲉⲛ ϯⲛⲟⲩ ⲉⲓ̈ⲣⲓ ⲛ ⲛⲓⲉⲛⲧⲟⲗⲏ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ Ⲫϯ ⲙⲡⲉⲣϫⲉ ϩⲗⲓ ⲙⲡⲁⲓⲣⲏϯ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲙⲡⲉⲣϫⲉⲙ ⲗⲱⲓϫⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛ ⲕⲟⲧⲥ ⲛⲧⲉ ϩⲁⲛ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ
51 1. You have seen the power of tears. You have seen confession. You have seen the fact of maintaining oneself and of aging in every sin. 2. Then, do not tell me: "I am a weak old man who has aged and I have sinned in every sin. I will not be able, from now on, to perform the commandments of God." Do not say anything in this way, do not find excuses regarding sins (Ps 141:4). Commandments are observed more during old age.
ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲕϫⲉⲙ ⲗⲱⲓϫⲓ ⲇⲉ ϯⲛⲁⲧⲥⲁⲃⲟⲕ ϯⲛⲟⲩ ϫⲉ ⲁⲕϣⲁⲛⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲙⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲭⲛⲁϣϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲉⲁⲣⲉϩ ⲉⲛⲓⲟⲩⲁϩ ⲥⲁϩⲛⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲭ̅ⲥ̅ ϧⲉⲛ
52 1. If you find (this) excuse, now I will teach you: If only you will want to, you will be able to observe the commandments of Christ in the time of your old age better than


[p. 498] {#po.52.p498}
ⲡⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲉⲧⲉⲕⲙⲉⲧⲁⲗⲟⲩ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲡⲁⲛⲧⲱⲥ ⲭⲛⲁϫⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲓ ϫⲉ ⲙⲟⲓ ⲛⲏⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲥⲁϫⲓ ⲁϭⲛⲉ ⲙⲉⲑⲛⲟⲩϫ ⲱⲟⲩ ⲛⲓⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓϩⲏⲕⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓ ⲡⲛ̅ⲁ̅ ϫⲉ ⲑⲱⲟⲩ ⲧⲉ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲓⲫⲏⲟⲩⲓ ⲡⲁⲛⲧⲱⲥ ⲭⲛⲁϫⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲓ ϫⲉ ⲉϣⲁⲣⲉⲡⲉⲛⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲑⲉⲃⲓϧ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲥⲏⲟⲩ ⲛⲧⲉⲛⲙⲉⲧⲃⲉⲣⲓ ϣⲁⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉⲛⲙⲉⲧϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ϥϫⲱ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲱⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ ⲓⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲏ ⲉⲧϩⲟⲕⲉⲣ ϯⲛⲟⲩ · ϫⲉ ⲛ̅ ⲑⲱⲟⲩ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲟⲩⲛⲁϯ ϩⲟ ⲉⲣⲱⲟⲩ ⲁϫⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲓ ϯⲛⲟⲩ ϫⲉ ⲁϣ ⲛⲉⲑⲙⲟⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ ⲡⲓϩⲏⲃⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙ‐ ⲱⲟⲩⲓ̈ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲃⲉⲣⲓ ⲧⲉ ϣⲁⲛ ϯⲙⲉⲧϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲧⲉ ⲱⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲓⲁⲧⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓ‐ ⲣⲉⲙⲣⲁⲩϣ ϩⲁⲣⲁ ⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲉⲣ ⲑⲙⲟⲗⲟⲅⲓⲛ ⲁⲛ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ϫⲉ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲗⲟⲩ ⲛⲁϣⲧ ⲉⲡⲓϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲥⲗⲟⲃϣ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛϫⲱⲛⲧ ϯⲙⲉⲧϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲇⲉ ⲟⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲣⲉⲙⲣⲁⲩϣ ⲉⲡⲓϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲉϣⲱⲡ ⲭⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲉⲉⲙⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲱⲛϩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ
(in that) of your youth. 2. Surely you will tell me: "Give me the word without falsehood." "Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the Kingdom of heaven." (Mt 5:3) Certainly you will ask me: "Is our thought humble at the time of our youth or during our old age?". Indeed, he says: "Blessed are those who hunger now, for they will be the ones to be comforted." (Mt 5:6, 4) Now tell me who is inclined to affliction and tears? Youth or old age? "Blessed are the meek!" (Mt 5:5) Then will we all not recognize that youth is harsher and is (more) hot toward anger? Old age, instead, is meeker. 3. If you will want to know (all this) clearly, you will find old age as your collaborator regarding all the commandments more than youth.
ⲭⲛⲁϫⲓⲙⲓ ⲛϯⲙⲉⲧϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲉⲥⲟⲓ ⲛϣⲫⲏⲣ ⲛⲉⲣ ϩⲱⲃ ⲛⲉⲙⲁⲕ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲉⲛ‐ ⲧⲟⲗⲏ ⲧⲏⲣⲟⲩ ⲉϩⲟⲧⲉ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲗⲟⲩ ⲉⲛ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲗⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ϣⲁⲣⲉϫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲥⲱⲙⲁ ϭⲉⲣⲟ ⲛ̀ⲣⲣⲏⲓ ⲛϧⲏⲧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϧⲉⲛ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲗⲟⲩ ϣⲁⲣⲉⲡϣⲁϩ ⲛϯⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓϩⲁⲧ ⲉⲣ ⲡⲟⲗⲉⲙⲓⲛ ⲛⲉⲙⲁⲛ ⲛⲉⲙ ϩⲁⲛ ⲕⲉⲙⲏϣ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲑⲟⲥ ⲉⲧⲉ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲱⲟⲩ ⲉⲧϣⲟⲩⲓⲧ ⲧⲉ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲗⲁⲣⲏⲧϥ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲣⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈
53 1. In youth, indeed, the thought of bodies blazes within us. In youth the fire of the love for money fights against us and (so also) many other passions such as vainglory, gluttony, the memory of offenses and greed.
ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲡⲉⲧϩⲱⲟⲩ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲧⲟⲓ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲑϧ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲕⲁⲧⲁ ⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲉϣⲁⲣⲉⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲃ̅ ⲁⲓⲁⲓ ⲉϣⲱⲡ ⲁⲡⲓⲟⲩⲁⲓ ⲉⲣ ⲁⲧϫⲟⲙ ⲡⲁⲛⲧⲱⲥ ⲥⲉⲛⲁϫⲓⲙⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲕⲉⲟⲩⲁⲓ ⲉϥϫⲉⲙ ϫⲟⲙ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲓⲣⲏϯ ϣⲁⲣⲉϯⲉⲡⲓⲑⲩⲙⲓⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲧϫⲟⲙ ⲛ̀ϯⲥⲁⲣⲝ ⲉⲣ ⲁⲡⲁⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛ̀ⲥⲉⲉⲣ ϧⲉⲗⲗⲟ ⲉϫⲉⲛ ϯⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲥⲱⲙⲁ ⲉϣⲱⲡ ϩⲟⲗⲱⲥ
54 1. And as when two men grow (together): if one becomes weak, surely the other will be considered stronger; in the same way, the desire and the power of the flesh grow and age in the soul and in the body. 2. In short, if the soul wants, it keeps the observance.


[p. 500] {#po.52.p500}
ⲁⲣⲉϣⲁⲛϯⲫⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ⲛ̀ⲧⲁⲥ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϫⲓ ⲛⲁ̀ⲣⲉϩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲓⲉⲛ‐ ⲧⲟⲗⲏ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲛϩⲟⲩⲟ ⲇⲉ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓ ⲙ̅ⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ
of the holy commandments (Rom 7:12), especially through tears and compunction.

### Discernment of tears
ⲉⲓϫⲱ ⲇⲉ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲕⲁⲧⲁ Ⲫϯ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ϫⲉ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ϯⲫⲩⲥⲓⲥ ϣⲟⲡ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉϣⲁⲩⲣⲓⲙⲓ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ϩⲁⲛⲟⲩⲟⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲕⲉⲛⲟⲇⲟⲝⲓⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲥⲁⲧⲁⲛⲁⲥ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲇⲉ ⲟⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲑⲓϧⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲁϣⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲧⲣⲟⲫⲏ ⲉⲧϭⲛ̅ϩⲟⲩⲧ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲟⲛ ⲉⲩⲧⲟⲩⲃⲏⲟⲩⲧ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ϯϩⲟϯ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ Ⲫϯ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲉⲣ ⲫⲙⲉⲩⲓ̈ ⲙ̅ⲫⲙⲟⲩ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲛⲓⲕⲟⲗⲁⲥⲓⲥ ⲉⲑⲛⲁϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲁⲛϣⲁⲛⲱⲥⲕ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉⲛⲣⲓⲥⲓ ⲛϧⲏⲧⲟⲩ ⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲉⲣ ⲡⲣⲟⲕⲟⲡⲧⲓⲛ ⲟⲩⲟⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩ ⲟⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡ̅ⲛ̅ⲁ̅ⲧⲓⲕⲟⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩϩⲟϯ ⲁⲛ ·̅ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ Ⲫϯ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲧⲱⲃϩ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩϫⲓⲛⲉⲣ ⲟⲩⲱⲓⲛⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲑⲉⲗⲏⲗ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ
55 1. I am referring to tears according to God, 2. since those that some weep for vainglory and for the love of Satan are tears of nature. 3. There are also tears (originating) from drunkenness and from a multitude of liquid foods. 4. And there are still pure tears (coming) from the fear of God, from the thought of death and the punishments that will be for us. If we remain in these (tears) and afflict ourselves in them, we will make progress. 5. There are still spiritual tears, not from fear, but from desire for God, (caused) by consolation, by illumination, and (by) joy of the Holy Spirit. Tears for the desire of God
ⲡⲓ ⲡⲛ̅ⲁ̅ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲁϥⲣⲓⲙⲓ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ Ⲫϯ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲟⲩⲱϣ ⲉϥⲟⲩⲁⲃ ϧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲉⲣⲙ‐ ⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲓⲣⲏϯ ⲛ̀ϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲡⲣⲟⲫⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲫⲁⲓ ϥϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲁϥϧⲟⲙϧⲉⲙ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲡⲁϩⲏⲧ ⲥⲁⲣϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲩϣⲱⲃⲧ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲛⲁϭⲗⲱⲧ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲟⲛ ϫⲉ ⲁⲡⲁϩⲏⲧ ϣⲑⲟⲣⲧⲉⲣ Ⲫϯ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲁϩⲏⲧ ⲧⲁⲧⲟⲓ ⲡⲉ Ⲫϯ ϣⲁ ⲉⲛⲉϩ ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ϭⲓ ϣϣⲱⲟⲩ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲥⲙⲟⲛⲕ ⲉⲓ ⲉⲣⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲛⲓⲁⲩⲗⲏⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡϭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲡⲁϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲧⲁⲥⲁⲣⲝ̅ ⲁⲩⲑⲉⲗⲏⲗ
56 1. Before God, with holy desire, the holy prophet wept such tears. 2. For this reason he says: "My heart was broken within me and my kidneys were changed" (Ps 72:21). And again: "My heart was troubled. The God of my heart is my portion, God forever" (Ps 72:26). "My soul is anxious and pines to enter the courts of the Lord" (Ps 83:3a), but "my heart and my flesh have exulted in the living


[p. 502] {#po.52.p502}
ⲉϫⲉⲛ Ⲫϯ ⲉⲧⲟⲛϩ ⲉϥϣⲟⲡ ⲛ̀ϫⲉ ⲡⲁⲓⲙⲉⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲓⲣⲏϯ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲣⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲯⲁⲗⲓⲛ ⲡⲉϫⲁϥ ϫⲉ ⲙ̅ⲫⲣⲏϯ ⲛ̅ ⲟⲩⲉⲓⲟⲩⲗ ⲉϥϭⲓ ϣϣⲱⲟⲩ ⲉϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲉϫⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛⲙⲟⲩⲙⲓ ⲙ̅ⲙⲱⲟⲩ ⲡⲁⲓⲣⲏϯ ⲥϭⲓ ϣϣⲱⲟⲩ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲧⲁⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲉⲓ̈ ϩⲁⲣⲟⲕ Ⲫϯ
God." (Ps 83:3b) 3. With this love in his heart and soul, the Psalmist said: "As a deer that longs to reach springs of water, so my soul longs to go toward you, O (my) God." (Ps 41:2).

### The work of the Holy Spirit in us
ⲙⲏⲡⲱⲥ ⲥⲉⲟⲓ ⲛⲁⲧⲉⲙⲓ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ϩⲁⲛⲟⲩⲟⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲑⲏⲛⲟⲩ ⲉⲛⲏ ⲉⲧⲉⲛϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲱⲟⲩ ⲕⲉⲡⲉⲣ ⲥⲉⲙ̅ⲡϣⲁ ⲁⲛ ⲟⲩⲇⲉ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲟⲩϩⲱⲃ ⲁⲛ ⲉϥϣⲟⲡ ϧⲉⲛ ⲫⲛⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲛϫⲱ ⲛ̅ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛⲱⲧⲉⲛ “ ⲟⲩ ⲁⲛ ⲉⲩϯ ⲙⲡⲟⲩⲥⲱⲓⲧ ϫⲉ ⲉϣⲁⲩϫⲉⲙⲟⲩ ⲣϧⲁⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓ ⲃⲁⲣⲃⲁⲣⲟⲥ ⲉⲩϫⲏϥ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲉⲛⲥⲁϫⲓ ⲛⲉⲙⲱⲧⲉⲛ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲟⲩϩⲱⲃ ⲉϥⲭⲏ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲥⲁϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲡⲉⲧⲉⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁⲩⲧⲏⲓϥ ⲉⲧⲟⲧⲉⲛ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲡⲭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲉϫⲟϥ ⲛⲱⲧⲉⲛ ⲉⲣⲉϯⲙⲉⲧⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲛⲧⲉ ⲛⲓⲫⲏⲟⲩⲓ ⲣⲁⲑⲟⲩⲉⲛ ⲡⲓ ⲡⲛ̅ⲁ̅ ⲉⲑⲟⲩⲁⲃ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉⲛ‐ ⲥⲁⲣϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲉϥϫⲫⲟ ⲛⲁⲛ ⲛⲟⲩⲙ ⲕⲁϩ ⲛϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲉⲙ ϩⲁⲛ ⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲛⲁϩⲣⲉⲛ
57 1. Perhaps some of you are ignorant regarding what we are saying, even if you are not worthy. 2. We are not telling you this regarding a thing that is found in the abyss of the earth, nor regarding some famous (and) rare species because they are found among the barbarians, 3. but we are speaking with you regarding a thing that is placed inside our heart (and) that has been granted to us by Christ to tell you: "The Kingdom of heaven with the Holy Spirit is within us" (Lk 17:21; Gal 4:6), generating in us compunction, tears toward the remission, renewal, and redemption of our sins. Three exemplary stories

### Greatness of tears and of the compassion of God
ⲟⲩϫⲓⲛⲭⲱ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲧⲃⲉⲣⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲥⲱϯ ⲛ̀ⲧⲉ ⲛⲉⲛ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ⲟⲩⲛⲓϣϯ ϫⲉ ϯⲛⲟⲩ ⲧⲉ ⲧϫⲟⲙ ⲛ̅ⲛⲓⲉⲣⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ⲕⲁⲧⲁ Ⲫϯ ⲟⲩⲛⲓϣϯ ⲧⲉ ⲧⲟⲩⲉⲛⲉⲣⲅⲓⲁ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲥⲉⲉⲣ ⲙⲉⲑⲣⲉ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲅⲣⲁⲫⲏ ϧⲉⲛ ϯⲡⲁⲗⲉⲁ ⲙⲉⲛ ⲕⲛϫⲉϩ ⲛⲓⲣⲉⲙ{ⲛⲓⲣⲉⲙ}ⲛⲓⲛⲉⲩⲏ Ⲓⲉ̅ⲍⲉⲕⲓⲁⲥ ⲛⲁⲃⲟⲩⲭⲟ‐ ⲇⲟⲛⲟⲥⲟⲣ
58 1. Great, now, is the power of tears according to God! Great is their strength! 2. And in the Scriptures, first in the Old, the men of Nineveh (Jon 3:6-10), Hezekiah (2 Kgs 20:1-6), Nebuchadnezzar (Dan 4:24, 31-37), and

### Manasseh
ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ ⲟⲩⲁⲧϣⲥⲁϫⲓ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲟⲩⲁⲧϣⲧⲁϩⲟϥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲧϣϫⲉⲙ ⲁⲩⲣⲏϫϥ ⲡⲉ ⲡⲓϣⲱⲕ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲟⲩⲱϣⲥ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲁϣⲁⲓ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲡⲉⲗⲁⲅⲟⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲓⲙⲉⲧϣⲉⲛ ϩⲏⲧ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ Ⲫϯ
(2 Chr 33:1-20) offer testimony. 3. Indescribable, unreachable, and limitless are the depth, the breadth, the quantity, and the sea of the compassions of God! Manasseh
ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ ⲁϥⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ⲛⲓⲙ ⲗⲟⲓⲡⲟⲛ ⲉⲑⲛⲁⲉⲣ ⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲛ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉϥⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲧⲏⲣⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲛⲧⲉϥϣⲧⲉⲙⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ⲁⲣϧⲟⲕ
59 1. Manasseh was saved thanks to his repentance. Who, then, will repent with all his soul and then not be saved? 2. Why do you say to me:


[p. 504] {#po.52.p504}
ⲉⲕϫⲱ ⲙⲙⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲓ ϫⲉ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲡⲟⲣⲛⲉⲩⲓⲛ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲛⲱⲓⲕ ⲁⲓ ϧⲱⲧⲉⲃ ⲁⲓϭⲓⲟⲩⲓ̈ ⲁⲓⲉⲣ ⲁⲡⲁⲥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲛⲁⲁⲛⲟⲙⲓⲁ ⲙⲙⲟⲛ ϣϫⲟⲙ ⲙⲙⲟⲓ ⲉⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲁϧⲟⲕ ⲉⲕϫⲱ ⲛ̅ⲛⲁⲓ: ⲓⲥ ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ ⲁⲪϯ ϣⲟⲡϥ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ϩⲟⲙⲱⲥ ϯⲛⲁⲉⲣ ⲧⲟⲗⲙⲁⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁϫⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲛⲑⲟϥ ϩⲱϥ ⲟⲛ ⲡⲓⲇⲓⲁⲃⲟⲗⲟⲥ ⲉⲛⲉⲉⲧⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲉϥⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲧⲏⲣⲥ ⲛⲁϥⲛⲁⲭⲱ ⲛⲁϥ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ
"I have fornicated, I have committed adultery, I have killed, I have stolen, I have grown old in my iniquities, I cannot be saved"? Why do you say these things? 3. Behold Manasseh: God accepted him to Himself thanks to his repentance. In any case, I would dare to say that (God) would have forgiven even the Devil himself if he had repented with all his soul.
ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲛⲟⲃⲓ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩ ⲛ̀ϫⲉ ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ: ⲁϥⲑⲣⲉ ⲫⲗⲁⲟⲥ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓ̅ⲥ̅ⲗ̅ ϣⲉⲙϣⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁϥⲟⲩⲱϣⲧ ⲛ̅ⲛⲓⲓⲇⲱⲗⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ ⲃ̅ ⲛ̅ ⲣⲟⲙⲡⲓ ⲁϫⲟⲥ ⲛⲏⲓ ϫⲉ ⲁⲟⲩⲏⲣ ⲛ̅ ⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲙⲟⲩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲃ̅ ⲛ̅ ⲣⲟⲙⲡⲓ ⲉⲧⲉⲙ‐ ⲙⲁⲩ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲩϣⲉ ⲉⲡⲧⲁⲕⲟ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲧⲱⲟⲩⲛ ϧⲁ ⲡⲟⲩϩⲁⲡ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ ⲡⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲭⲣⲉⲱⲥⲧⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲉϥϭⲣⲟ ⲉⲡⲓ ⲛⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ Ⲫϯ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲧⲉϥⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲉϥϧⲉⲛ ⲧⲁⲓⲛⲓϣϯ ⲙ̅ⲙⲉⲧⲁⲙⲉⲗⲉⲥ
60 1. In what did Manasseh sin? (He) induced all the people of Israel to worship idols and he (himself, in turn,) venerated them for fifty-two years (2 Chr 33:1-9). 2. Now tell me: "How many men died during those fifty-two years and (how many) went to ruin, of those over whom Manasseh, the king, assumed judgment?". 3. And being a debtor, he did not triumph over the mercy of God or over His philanthropy, even while finding himself in such great negligence.
ⲙ̅ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲣⲏϯ ⲥⲉϫⲱ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲛⲓⲓⲥⲧⲟⲣⲓⲟⲅⲣⲁⲫⲟⲥ ⲛ̅ⲁⲣⲭⲉⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ ⲡⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲙ̅ ⲡⲓ̅ⲥ̅ⲗ̅ ⲁⲩⲉⲣ ⲉⲭⲙⲁⲗⲱⲧⲉⲩⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟϥ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲛⲓⲭⲁⲗⲇⲉⲟⲥ ⲉⲑⲃⲁⲃⲩⲗⲱⲛ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ϯⲡⲉⲣⲥⲓⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲩϩⲓⲧϥ ⲉⲣϧⲟⲩⲛ ⲉⲟⲩ ⲍⲱⲧⲓⲟⲛ ⲛ̅ϩⲟⲙⲧ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲡⲟⲩⲣⲟ ⲛ̅ ⲛⲓⲡⲉⲣⲥⲏⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁϥⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲉϥϣⲟⲡ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲁⲓ ⲍⲱⲧⲓⲟⲛ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲓ ⲣⲏϯ ⲁϥϣⲗⲏⲗ ⲅⲁⲣ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ϩⲁⲛ ⲉⲣ‐ ⲙⲱⲟⲩⲓ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲧⲱⲃϩ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲧⲉϥϩⲱⲇⲏ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲟⲩⲁϩ ⲥⲁϩⲛⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ Ⲫϯ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲧⲉϥⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ ⲁϥⲫⲱⲣ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲡⲓⲍⲱⲧⲓⲟⲛ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁϥⲓ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ ⲁϥⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ϩⲓⲧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲁⲅⲅⲉⲗⲟⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁϥⲓ
61 1. Indeed, ancient historiographers report that Manasseh, king of Israel, was taken prisoner by the Chaldeans in Babylon in Persia (2 Chr 33:1-20) and was placed inside a bronze animal statue by the king of the Persians. Then, while he was in this statue, he was saved thus: 2. through tears, in fact, he prayed with the supplication of his song. And so, by order of God and by His philanthropy, the statue broke, Manasseh came out and was saved by an angel; then he returned to Jerusalem and died there, remaining in repentance and confession.
ⲉⲓ̅ⲗ̅ⲏ̅ⲙ̅ ⲁϥⲙⲟⲩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲓⲙⲁ ⲉⲧⲉⲙⲙⲁⲩ ⲉϥϣⲟⲡ ϧⲉⲛ ϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ϯⲉⲝⲟⲙⲟⲗⲟⲅⲓⲥⲓⲥ Ⲙ̅ ⲡⲓϣⲱⲕ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲡⲉⲗⲁⲅⲟⲥ ⲛ̀ⲁⲧⲥⲁϫⲓ ⲉⲣⲟϥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲛⲓⲙⲉⲧϣⲉⲛ ϩⲏⲧ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲛⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲓⲁⲅⲁⲑⲟⲥ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ Ⲫϯ ⲫⲁⲓ ⲉⲧⲛⲟϩⲉⲙ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉⲧⲑⲙⲁⲓⲟ ϫⲓⲛϫⲏ ⲛ̅ⲛⲏ ⲉⲑⲛⲁⲫⲱⲧ ϩⲁⲣⲟϥ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲙⲉⲑⲙⲏⲓ
62 1. O depth and indescribable sea of the compassions and of the mercy of the only Good and Philanthropic God who saves and who in truth justifies freely those who will seek refuge in Him!


[p. 506] {#po.52.p506}
ⲟⲩ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲡⲉ ⲉⲧⲁϥⲉⲛϥ ϣⲁⲣⲟϥ ϫⲉ ⲙⲁⲛⲁⲥⲥⲏ ⲉϥⲉⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲛ̅ⲧϣⲉⲃⲓⲱ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓϩⲁⲡ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓⲧⲁⲕⲟ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ⲡⲁⲓⲑⲃⲁ ⲙ̅ ⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲙ̅ⲡⲁⲓ ⲣⲏϯ ⲉⲧⲁⲩϩⲓⲧⲟⲩ ⲉⲡϩⲁⲡ ⲛ̅ϯⲅⲉⲉⲛⲛⲁ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁⲩⲥⲱⲣⲉⲙ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧϥ ⲙ̅ ⲡⲉ‐ ϥⲉⲛ ϩⲗⲓ ⲁⲗⲗⲁ ⲉⲡⲓⲇⲏ ⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡⲉϥϩⲏⲧ ⲧⲏⲣϥ ⲉⲁϥⲫⲱⲧ ⲉⲣⲁⲧϥ ⲙ̅Ⲫϯ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲁϥⲭⲁ ϩⲑⲏϥ ⲙ̅ⲙⲁⲩⲁⲧϥ ⲉⲛⲉϥⲙⲉⲧϣⲉⲛ ϩⲏⲧ ⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲡⲉⲙⲡϣⲁ ⲉⲃⲟⲗ ϩⲓⲧⲟⲧϥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉϥⲙⲉⲧⲙⲁⲓⲣⲱⲙⲓ
2. Indeed, what did Manasseh offer Him that was worthy of changing the judgment and of (causing) the destruction of these ten thousand souls who threw themselves toward the judgment of Gehenna and were lost through him? He brought nothing, 3. but then he repented with all his heart, having taken refuge at the feet of God: and he entrusted his heart exclusively to His compassion and by Him was made worthy of His philanthropy.

### A young brigand
ⲓⲥϫⲉ ⲭⲟⲩⲱϣ ϯⲛⲁⲭⲱ ⲉⲣⲟⲕ ⲛ̅ ⲕⲉ ⲍⲏⲧⲓⲙⲁ ⲛ̅ⲓⲥⲧⲟⲣⲓⲁ ⲉⲩⲧⲟⲩ‐ ⲛⲟⲥ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲛ ⲉϩⲣⲏⲓ ⲉϯⲙⲉⲧⲁⲛⲟⲓⲁ ⲛⲉⲙ ⲡⲓϫⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲁⲥⲑⲟⲛ ϩⲁ Ⲫϯ ⲁϥⲥⲣⲁⲓ ⲅⲁⲣ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲕⲗⲏⲙⲏⲥ ⲡⲓⲣⲉϥⲉⲣ ⲓⲥⲧⲟⲣⲓⲛ ⲛ̅ ⲣⲉⲙⲣⲁⲕⲟϯ ⲉⲑⲃⲉ ⲓⲱⲁⲛⲛⲏⲥ ⲡⲓⲑⲉⲟⲗⲟⲅⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ⲉϥⲙⲟϣⲓ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲓⲱⲁⲛⲛⲏⲥ ⲡⲓⲁ̀‐ ⲡⲟⲥⲧⲟⲗⲟⲥ ⲫⲙⲁⲑⲏⲧⲏⲥ ⲙ̅ⲡⲭ̅ⲥ̅ ⲉϥϩⲓ ⲱⲓϣ ϧⲉⲛ ⲟⲩⲡⲟⲗⲓⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ϯⲁⲥⲓⲁ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡϫⲓⲛⲑⲣⲉϥⲛⲁⲩ ⲉⲟⲩⲁⲗⲟⲩ ⲉϥⲟⲓ ⲛ̅ϫⲱⲣⲓ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ⲉϥⲥⲉⲗ‐ ⲥⲱⲗ ϧⲉⲛ ⲑⲙⲉⲧⲥⲁⲓ̈ ⲉⲛⲉϥⲡⲣⲟⲥⲱⲡⲟⲛ ⲉϥⲟⲩⲁϣ ⲡⲟⲩϫⲁⲓ ⲛ̅ⲧⲯⲩⲭⲏ ⲙ̅ⲫⲁⲓ ⲁϥϯ ⲥⲃⲱ ⲛⲁϥ ⲉⲑⲣⲉϥϣⲱⲡⲓ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲥⲧⲟⲥ ⲟⲩⲟϩ ϧⲉⲛ ⲡϫⲓⲛ‐ ⲑⲣⲉϥⲑⲉⲧ ⲡϩⲏⲧ ⲙ̅ⲡⲓⲁⲗⲟⲩ ⲁϥⲟⲗϥ ϣⲁ ⲡⲓⲉⲡⲓⲥⲕⲟⲡⲟⲥ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ ϯⲡⲟⲗⲓⲥ ⲁϥⲉⲣ ⲕⲁⲑⲏⲅⲓⲛ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟϥ ⲛ̅ϫⲉ ⲓⲱⲁⲛⲛⲏⲥ ⲉϥϫⲱ ⲙ̅ⲙⲟⲥ ϫⲉ ϯⲭⲱ ϧⲁⲧⲟⲧⲕ ⲙ̅ ϯⲉⲕⲕⲗⲏⲥⲓⲁ ⲧⲏⲣⲥ ⲉⲑⲣⲉⲕϯ ⲥⲃⲱ ⲛⲁϥ ϧⲉⲛ ϯϩⲟϯ ⲛ̅ⲧⲉ Ⲫϯ “ ⲛⲁⲓ
63 1. If you wish, I will set forth other historical researches that arouse our repentance and our conversion to God. 2. The Alexandrian historian Clement, in fact, wrote (these things) regarding John the theologian. 3. John the apostle, the disciple of Christ, going from place to place (and) preaching in a city of Asia, as soon as he saw a vigorous boy adorned with beauty in his face, desiring the salvation of his soul, instructed him so that he might become a believer. 4. And after having convinced the heart of the boy, (then) John presented him to the bishop of the city, informing him with these words: "O bishop, God and all the Church are witnesses, I entrust this boy to you so that you may instruct him in the fear of God." 5. After having said this, John departed, arrived in another region, and (continued) to preach there.
